## Solana source audit: blocking privacy and persistence gaps — 2026-10-14

Following the human priority order, resumed Solana source review after Mongo graph rollback/replay work. This is source evidence, not a fresh live-chain certification:

- Active activation (`SolanaOasis.AvatarLoad1.cs`, line60) constructs `SolanaRepository`. `SaveAvatarAsync` (`SolanaOasis.AvatarLoad2.cs`, lines179/185) calls its Update/Create methods.
- `Extensions/OasisEntitiesExtensions.cs` maps actual avatar Email and Password into `SolanaAvatarDto`. The DTO also includes username, names and metadata. Repository `EncodeEntity` serializes the DTO, gzip-compresses it and base64-encodes it with `oasis:gzip:`. Create/Update publish that value through MemoProgram.NewMemoV2. Compression is not encryption: this path does not implement the approved encrypted-private-record policy. Do not submit real avatar data to a public network to verify it. No evidence gathered here establishes that real user data was previously published.
- Repository Create returns an empty string when the blockhash result is null; Update/Delete dereference the blockhash result. Native submission acknowledgment is followed by a fixed three-second delay, not verified final transaction outcome. Failure/uncertainty classification and replay safety require implementation and actual native tests.
- SaveAvatar's update branch loads the existing DTO then writes that DTO back without applying the supplied avatar changes. Successful submission therefore cannot prove updated profile persistence. SaveAvatarDetail and holon counterparts need the same audit.
- Import loops individual saves and reports success if any save succeeds. It is not atomic, loses individual native causes in warning messages and needs an explicit partial-mutation/no-replay contract before V2 can safely fail over.
- Avatar export queries program accounts using the configured OASIS account key, while the repository stores DTOs as transaction memos. These are different storage models; export/query fidelity is not established by successful RPC calls. Other service helpers synthesize `.example`/`.local` emails and must be audited rather than counted as truthful storage reads.

Required next: reuse the existing private-record protection/key persistence and blinded-index design rather than invent another encryption system; connect it to the actual Solana write/read owner, prove email/username lookup without public disclosure, fix canonical update mapping, classify native finality/uncertainty, and verify operations through the retained official validator with synthetic data only. ### Cosmos/Sui explicit off-chain NFT projection — 2026-10-13

Closed the blanket `StoreNFTMetaDataOnChain=false` rejection using shared `NftMetadataProjection`, not another uploader or guessed external-JSON parser. Off-chain mode requires an absolute HTTP(S)/IPFS/Arweave URI without userinfo. The existing manager remains responsible for uploads and URI preparation. Providers serialize only a whitelist of identity/collection/standard/provider/wallet/recipient/URI fields in a Web3NFT-compatible envelope; description, JSON payload, images, avatar IDs, tags, economics and custom metadata (including legacy query-exempt keys) are not copied into that projection. The URI and native wallet/identity information are public; do not supply secret-bearing URLs. Default/null and explicit true retain existing on-chain mode. No external URI is fetched, so no new SSRF/request-authentication surface was added.

Cosmos returns its original request metadata in the mint response while storing the projection. Sui binds readback GUID/creator/owner and off-chain flag/URI before returning the corresponding original submitted record; standalone `LoadOnChainNFTData` deliberately returns the stored identity/URI projection, not purported externally hydrated metadata. External presentation JSON still is not a Web3NFT document. This does not prove marketplace royalty enforcement, arbitrary URI availability, live-chain support, or production API deployment.

## Routing contract

### Off-chain NFT URI pre-mint ordering confirmed — 2026-10-12

- `NFTManager.Wallets2.MintNFTInternalAsync` performs JSON upload/ExternalJSONURL selection before mint dispatch, forces individual provider NumberToMint=1, then assigns `web3Request.JSONMetaDataURL=mergedRequest.JSONMetaDataURL`. Thus off-chain URI is already available to provider minting; no parallel uploader/pre-mint ID guess is needed.
- Cosmos retained asset schema stores `metadata_json` string, while current provider readback deserializes that string as Web3NFT metadata. Proper StoreNFTMetaDataOnChain=false support needs a defined minimal pointer representation and resolution behavior compatible with NFTManager's existing metadata format/access rules, rather than publishing the full record or returning an unexplained partial object. Removing the existing rejection alone is not completion.
- Next implementation gate: trace manager-generated external JSON shape and its authorized load flow, implement one shared pointer/readback contract reused by Cosmos/Sui, test manager-to-provider mint/load including identity and untrusted URI handling. No schema/contract deployment, funds, SDK operation or speculative alternate flow changed in this audit.


### Existing NFTManager off-chain metadata integration points — 2026-10-12

- Source trace locates existing off-chain mode handling in ONODE Core `NFTManager.Wallets2.cs`: Pinata/IPFS/Arweave/OASIS upload modes and ExternalJSONURL validation. `NFTManager.Wallets.cs` merges nullable Web3 overrides, branches on StoreNFTMetaDataOnChain and forwards NFTOffChainMetaType/Store flag; `NFTManager.MintLoad.cs` also forwards these parent WEB4 fields into provider requests.
- Cosmos/Sui current provider code does not independently handle off-chain mode fields and rejects StoreNFTMetaDataOnChain=false. Proper support must reuse the manager's existing URI/storage workflow, with explicit URI availability/order, asset metadata representation and authorized readback contract. Do not add provider-specific parallel upload systems or claim mode forwarding is implementation proof.
- Required next trace/test: follow the exact mint execution ordering and provider result ID/locator through JSON upload and persistence, then implement off-chain asset linkage/readback consistently. External metadata URLs also need existing access/security constraints retained. Full operation/option and production recovery gates remain open; no production change/SDK/network/funds/install/deployment/merge in this source audit.


### NFT option persistence versus enforcement audit — 2026-10-12

- Cosmos `MintNFTAsync` validates provider/NFT-standard selection and numeric/date constraints, then copies price/discount/royalty/seller basis points/sale flags/dates into Web3NFT metadata serialized for `nft_mint`. These fields are persisted, not simply dropped. That C# path alone does not prove marketplace sale constraints or royalty enforcement by the contract/coordinator; those are separate semantics requiring contract and manager tests.
- Sui lock reads NFT metadata before its mutation; unlock retains committed digest when separate readback fails. The read/metadata operations cannot be treated as proof of every mint request option, royalty behavior or coordinator authorization. No speculative rejection or alternative mint path was added based on this partial audit.
- Required next option audit: trace each public request field through provider SDK, actual contract and NFTManager, classify persisted metadata versus enforced business rules, then implement/test necessary missing behavior. Existing rejection of off-chain-only mint metadata remains a functional gap rather than full support. No new runtime/funds/download/deployment/merge.

### TRON duplicate/P2P broadcast outcome classification — 2026-10-12

### Existing OASISDNA encryption architecture reconciliation — 2026-10-12

- Existing DNA `Security.AvatarPassword`, `OASISProviderPrivateKeys`, and `HolonDataEncryption` are distinct settings. Password helper supports BCrypt hashes wrapped by AES-256-CBC (`r256:`) and AES-256-GCM (`qpq:`). Wallet/key paths use reversible Rijndael plus optional quantum wrapping. BCrypt cannot reversibly encrypt private records. The helper's quantum layer is symmetric AES-GCM, not a separate post-quantum public-key algorithm.
- HolonManager applies configured reversible encryption to non-exempt metadata before provider storage. Queryable system keys/additional configured keys remain exempt, and ordinary record fields are not all covered by that metadata envelope. This manager-level path is upstream of Mongo and other providers; direct provider calls do not thereby gain full-record encryption.
- New Cosmos/Sui private backend envelopes are separate whole-record authenticated storage protection with keyed physical indexes. They currently do not reuse DNA encryption settings. Integration/reuse and configuration ownership remain an explicit open gate, not completed merely because isolated encryption/live routing tests pass. Do not remove mandatory private on-chain protection while resolving that architecture.
- New existing-helper compatibility tests **7/7 passed**, zero skips, `TestResults/SharedPrivate/dna-encryption-compatibility-current.trx`: BCrypt-only/password wrapping combinations and each reversible CBC/GCM data combination. These do not prove end-to-end Mongo or deployed configuration.
- Audit gap: enabled helper layers with missing keys can be silently skipped; `DecryptValue` and `UnwrapQuantumLayer` return original ciphertext on failures. Shared fail-closed semantics need implementation with manager/provider regression tests before claiming encryption complete. No shared production behavior was changed in this checkpoint.

### Sui/Cosmos private-record composition gap — 2026-10-12

Source audit confirms Sui and Cosmos constructors directly compose `SuiStorageBackend` / `CosmosSdkBackend`, while Hedera and TRON explicitly compose `PrivateKeyValueBackend`. The shared storage base serializes avatar/detail records and writes username/email/token indexes through its configured backend. Therefore Sui/Cosmos generic storage currently lacks the private-record wrapper at this composition boundary. This is an exposure risk in implementation, not evidence that a particular user's credentials were written; no user ledger records or secrets were inspected during the audit.

Required release gate: choose private/public record policy, reuse the existing private wrapper and one SDK transport, persist an independent encryption key without regeneration, and explicitly version/migrate existing plaintext records with verified readback and preserved recovery evidence. Blindly wrapping an existing ledger would make old records/indexes unreadable; silently trying plaintext after decryption failure would violate the canonical-format/no-fallback policy. Encryption of new/current values cannot erase historical plaintext from an immutable public ledger. Existing private wrapper also does not hide record counts/sizes/access patterns.

Requested the user's migration/privacy choice asynchronously; no storage format, credentials, ledger state, deployment or branch was changed. Passing real SDK/ledger tests are not private-storage certification. This newly identified gate prevents claiming complete safe provider support or merging the unfinished release.

### Account-operation audit: canonical provider versus legacy helper — 2026-10-12

Separate public **HashgraphClient** legacy methods in `HashgraphOASIS.cs` still have documented gaps:

| Legacy helper | Observed gap | Required future treatment |
|---|---|---|
| `CreateAccountAsync()` / `RestoreAccountAsync(string)` | NBitcoin BIP-39 seed truncated to 32 bytes with NaCl signing, Base64 key output; creation swallows exceptions and returns null; not canonical Hedera SDK derivation | Explicit versioned migration/compatibility decision; never silently change existing mnemonic-to-key mapping |
| `ResolveAccountIdFromKeysAsync` | Multiple guessed mirror query formats and silent unsuccessful-path handling | One documented mirror contract with truthful failures; no invented account identity |
| `GetTransactionStatusAsync` | Separate per-call HTTP client rather than provider's validated no-redirect mirror transport | Review endpoint/redirect policy and delegate through a defined boundary |

Source search found direct constructor references only in the helper itself and existing integration tests. This does not prove external/reflection callers cannot use the public helper. No legacy implementation was changed and no wallet material was transformed. These gaps are not proof that the canonical V2 provider account path is broken, but they also prevent a blanket claim that every public helper uses real SDKs. No tests, network spending, installation, deployment or merge performed during this source audit.

### Ordered preparation transport — 2026-10-12

The shared `PreparedSubmissionServer.ForStages` now accepts an explicit copied sequence of 2–16 distinct bounded original transaction IDs. This is the exact byte array signed and submitted, not a rebuilt replacement transaction or a response-derived identity. Preparation/signature failures remain pre-submission errors; transport exceptions after execution begins carry the precomputed digest and unknown outcome. Successful/failed response identity must match that digest before an execution outcome is accepted. Missing/mismatched receipts cannot replace the original identity, and no retry is introduced.

An `AsyncLocalStorage` operation scope preserves native evidence for later mapping/readback errors throughout the exported `invoke` operation. It is not global mutable last-transaction state; concurrent operation tests verify distinct original digests remain isolated. Known successful execution followed by mapping failure retains that execution evidence while the overall operation remains an error requiring reconciliation. SDK tests now use fully prepared official transaction objects, real keypair signatures and SDK signature verification at the submission seam. A real official gRPC call to an unavailable loopback endpoint also retains original signed identity with an unknown outcome; this was not a node that accepted a transaction, so it is not charged-outage proof.

### Hedera native process evidence and cancellation semantics (2026-10-12)

Hedera now exposes immutable `HederaNativeMutationEvidence` through reconciliation-required exceptions, including cancellation and timeout subclasses that retain normal `OperationCanceledException`/`TimeoutException` semantics. The Core replay guard can inspect these types through its existing interface instead of matching messages. IDs/stage/native status are retained as evidence; no execution success/failure or fee outcome is inferred from a status name. Process cancellation retains the caller token and explicitly unverified mutation/approval IDs. Ending the process does not cancel a possible ledger submission. Known construction/signing/validation failures without a completed approval remain ordinary errors; submission/receipt/approval/mutation/readback failures and malformed mutation error evidence stop replay. Non-mutation and explicit SDK preflight errors do not acquire that classification.

Storage/file write adapters now record construction/signing/submission/receipt stages around the existing official SDK call, preserving single submission and fixed IDs. They reuse the existing native-status extractor, including the known SDK attempt-limit format. C# native mutations require an explicit original ID before launching the SDK; an absent/blank ID is rejected before possible SDK-generated identity or submission. Structured-error parse failures are retained as inner exceptions rather than silently discarded, without exposing stderr/key material.

SDK regression across bridge/deployment/escrow/file/storage passed **85/85**, zero skips. No funded transfer/deployment, balance/faucet polling, native mint, runtime install or download. Original funded Hedera deployment/storage/NFT/V2 and broader native/API/billing gates remain open. ### Hedera duplicate-submission fee reconciliation (2026-10-12)

### Sui exact native fee receipts implemented and read-only live verified (2026-10-12)

Added real SDK `nativeFeeReceipt` and C# provider `GetNativeFeeReceiptAsync`: exact original digest/effects identity, explicit successful/failed status, checkpoint/time presence, actual signed gas owner, observed chain identifier, SUI/MIST denomination, canonical unsigned uint64 components and exact signed net units. Per [official Sui gas documentation](https://www.docs.sui.io/develop/transaction-payment/gas-in-sui), net charge is computation plus storage minus rebate; non-refundable storage is reported, not double-added. Negative net rebates are preserved, not fabricated as zero. Numeric/rounded components, malformed payer/digest or uncheckpointed evidence fail rather than inventing a cost.

### Native interface completion audit scope (2026-10-12)

Inspected current Core IOASISBlockchainStorageProvider and IOASISNFTProvider declarations. Beyond inherited provider/storage contracts, blockchain declares 21 methods: token send/mint/burn/lock/unlock, balance, transactions and key generation (eight sync/async pairs), then account create/restore, native bridge withdraw/deposit and transaction status (five async methods). NFT declares 16 methods: mint/send/burn/lock/unlock/load (six sync/async pairs), NFT bridge withdraw/deposit (two async), and collection resize (two default explicit unsupported methods). IOASISSmartContractProvider is a marker interface with no declared operations; generic contract helpers still require truthful implementation/testing, but cannot be inferred from marker membership. These source counts are inventory, not execution proof.

Required per-provider audit must distinguish: native/custom-token paths; sync wrappers versus asynchronous operations; real signing identity versus funded ledger account; exact native history versus approximate display; configured custody versus avatar-owned encrypted wallet; positive lifecycle versus authority rejection; committed native bridge leg versus full cross-chain settlement; and natively applicable collection resize versus unsupported capability. Default SetCollectionSize errors are permitted for genuinely inapplicable chains; do not fabricate resizing or mark supported without native capability evidence. Legacy gaps remain documentation only. Full certification requires method-by-method relevant evidence, not merely interface implementation/compiler success or no TODO search matches.

### Newly identified Hedera raw-address native-send gap (2026-10-12)

Source audit finds `HashgraphOASIS.SendTransactionAsync(fromWalletAddress,toWalletAddress,amount,memo)` constructs default HashgraphClient and calls its SendTransactionAsync, which POSTs unsigned application JSON to mirror `/api/v1/transactions`. It does not use configured consensus network/nodes or a signing key and cannot establish a real Hedera transfer. Provider response also contains a generic-success-text fallback for missing transaction identity. This is an implementation gap, distinct from working explicit-key SendToken/native transfer and avatar-wallet overloads. Required fix: reuse existing official SDK transfer execution, obtain an explicitly authorized signer matching sender, honor configured network/nodes, require actual receipt/original ID and propagate failures without fabricated identities or automatic replay. Do not silently use bridge custody for arbitrary senders. Existing HashgraphClient constructor contains account/key fields but current method ignores them. No mutation/funding/test pass for this route is claimed. This gap is part of full relevant native-interface gate and must be closed before merge.

### Open karma command ownership/replication audit (2026-10-11)

Source inspection identifies an unverified correctness gate: `OASISStorageProviderBase.AddKarmaToAvatar[Async]`/`RemoveKarmaFromAvatar[Async]` delegate to `IAvatarDetail.KarmaEarnt/KarmaLost`, whose concrete `AvatarDetail` implementation invokes the **global `KarmaManager.Instance`**, not provider-local persistence. KarmaManager then mutates its durable ledger and saves the detail projection. Meanwhile V2 `IsReplicationOperation` includes `AddKarmaToAvatar`/`RemoveKarmaFromAvatar`, so replica invocation may repeat a business command instead of copying its committed projection. This is a source-supported risk, **not yet a demonstrated duplicate-ledger mutation**. Required next proof: controlled submission-count test against the actual router/provider interface dispatch, then establish a single business-command owner and replicate only committed storage projection; verify no failover/resubmission can duplicate a committed reward. Do not implement a second karma calculation/ledger or claim full 42-operation correctness until this gate is resolved. No ledger mutation, installation, download or merge occurred during this audit.

### Explicit authoritative-absence contract implementation (2026-10-11)

Common error code `OASIS_RECORD_NOT_FOUND` identifies successful store reads that establish absence. Shared avatar ID/index/detail and holon loads retain direct/legacy `IsError=true` semantics but emit this code, propagating it through detail-index lookup. V2 conversion alone treats coded null record responses without an exception as terminal non-error null; payload-bearing, uncoded or exception-bearing failures are not normalized. No message parsing or mutation retries. ### Newly identified authoritative-not-found contract gap (2026-10-11)

Source audit identifies a remaining cross-provider correctness gate: `KeyValueStorageProviderBase.LoadAvatarAsync` and avatar-index/detail/holon reads return `Fail<T>` for authoritative absence. V2's documented terminal-not-found contract is instead a non-error null payload. `JwtMiddleware` maps every avatar-load `IsError` to HTTP 503, so a deleted Sui/shared-store avatar can be confused with a provider outage and trigger inappropriate failover. Existing shared test `Missing_avatar_is_an_error_not_an_empty_success` codifies the conflicting provider behavior; passing authentication tests only cover existing avatars and do not close this gap. No message-string parser, legacy fallback or silent retry is acceptable. Required next work is an explicit, consistent result contract that distinguishes authoritative absence from transport/corruption errors, preserves documented legacy characterization, and proves V2 terminal absence plus deleted-avatar HTTP rejection against real storage. This is source-confirmed discrepancy, not a newly executed deleted-avatar HTTP result. No production fix or completion claim is made in this audit.

### Shared token submission safety checkpoint (2026-10-11)

Unlock checkpoint: Core `687b6ba3bb542af4bbeee233f25189ba82f6b9c5` preserves `UnlockedByAvatarId` in both provider request projections and removes sync/async unlock resubmission loops. Provider receipt/error messages remain unchanged; absent response or thrown failures are explicit reconciliation errors. **18/18** controlled-provider submission regressions pass, zero skips, including eight unlock success/readback/null/throw cases verifying one invocation and exact owner/asset forwarding with the retry flag enabled. Core dual-framework build passed, zero errors/254 warnings. These supersede the pending unlock control-flow audit, not live custody/HTTP authorization gates. Manifest and parent gitlink advance together; no deploy/merge.

Lock checkpoint: Core `29c1bbfb398ef2c382b45d9958e85d681c3e91c7` removes the shared sync/async token-lock resubmission loops and preserves `LockedByAvatarId`, `FromWalletAddress` and `FromWalletPrivateKey`, previously omitted in the WEB4-to-provider projection. Original provider messages and reconciliation-bearing errors are retained. Expanded submission tests pass **10/10**, zero skips, with sync/async public locks verifying all projected asset/owner/signer fields and exactly one provider invocation after approval/readback failure. Core builds for both frameworks, zero errors/254 warnings. Unlock path auditing is still pending and not covered by this proof; burns already delegate once. Controlled mocks prove manager behavior, not funded custody or authenticated HTTP authorization. Manifest/gitlink updated together; no deployment or merge.

Regression checkpoint: **8/8 submission-count tests passed**, zero skips, in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/wallet-token-submission.trx`. With V2 and `WaitTillTokenSent=true`, controlled blockchain providers return success, a committed native digest plus readback error, null, or throw; the actual manager internal sync/async methods each invoke the provider exactly once. Original success/readback messages and digest-bearing response objects are preserved; null/throw cases are explicit errors. These are Moq control-flow tests, not live SDK/HTTP coverage. The older public sync avatar-address path has a separate preexisting misplaced `else` validation and has not been proven by this selection; that gap remains documented, not represented as tested or fixed.

The new Sui avatar overloads are concrete-provider convenience methods, not additions to `IOASISBlockchainStorageProvider`; HTTP requests still enter the shared `SendToken` request pipeline. Auditing that pipeline found unconditional payment resubmission loops controlled by `WaitTillTokenSent` in public sync and internal sync/async paths. A provider can commit a payment then fail custody/readback, so retrying that operation could transfer twice. Core commit `2fe3cdcd83bb5ca634101f44d039c48f8fec1540` removes those three loops: each path submits once, preserves provider errors/messages (including transaction reconciliation IDs), errors on a missing transaction response, and reports thrown errors without resubmission. Provider response messages are no longer overwritten with generic success. The retry flags no longer authorize payment resubmission; receipt/status reconciliation must precede an explicit new request. Core built for both target frameworks, zero errors/254 warnings. Dedicated submission-count regression tests and authenticated HTTP behavior are still pending; this build is not full verification. Parent gitlink and deployment manifest are advanced together; no deployment/merge performed.

The placeholder `0.0.123456`, fixed `Amount=1`, unsigned pool release and unused `_contractAddress` have been removed. Configuration, requesting avatar and numeric token ID are validated before wallet lookup. The requesting avatar's detached signing wallet is resolved with private keys; supplied lock wallet/key, if present, must match it. A bounded 20-second, no-redirect configured mirror lookup obtains the EVM address and checks active numeric account identity; the SDK independently corroborates that address through consensus account lookup before approval. Mirror data alone is not authorization. The amount-less request locks the full spendable ledger balance; unlock releases that owner's recorded deposit. Success requires the exact original mutation ID, consensus/custody success and positive int64 units in the SDK response. Provider build passed with zero errors/four warnings, and **9/9 C# token escrow validation tests passed**, zero skips (`TestResults/HederaRuntime/hedera-token-escrow-validation.trx`). BootLoader forwards them via `HederaTokenEscrowConfiguration`; the provider snapshots its values so later mutation of the supplied object cannot change its deployment selection. Validation requires nonzero numeric contract ID, exact code hash, positive safe-integer gas and positive int64 fee. Nothing silently reuses NFT configuration or supplies a default contract/fee. The token methods have **not yet been switched** to this configuration/executor, so this is configuration plumbing only, not completed C# escrow support. Subsequent execution checkpoint: the bridge dispatcher now routes `tokenEscrowLock`/`tokenEscrowUnlock` to real SDK signed execution. It requires an explicit sender EVM address verified by native account alias lookup, the active account's single signing key, and matching deployed bytecode hash. Contract EVM identity comes from `ContractInfo.contractAccountId` and is corroborated by native alias lookup, not guessed from its numeric ID. Contract calls read exact token balance, allowance and depositor-owned locked units; lock approves the full observed balance only when allowance is zero (a conflicting allowance errors), then verifies allowance and unchanged balance/deposit before mutation. Unlock releases the recorded deposit. Consensus receipts and exact post-mutation balance/deposit readback are required for success; failures retain original mutation and confirmed approval IDs for reconciliation. Client closure is guaranteed. **66/66 local SDK tests passed**, zero skips, including missing EVM identity and mismatched signer rejection before paid queries. These tests do not exercise funded SDK queries or submissions. C# wiring and funded ledger verification remain open; execution implementation is not live proof.

The existing pinned Hiero SDK supports exact `bigint` token allowances; no installation or download was needed. `SdkBridge/escrow.mjs` now constructs the combined escrow contract's `lockAll(address)` and `unlock(address)` calls and native HTS fungible allowances. Common account, runtime-hash, gas, fee and payer validation is shared with NFT escrow; NFT serial validation remains NFT-specific. Approval uses a separate transaction ID for the same payer, and both mutations and approvals disable ID regeneration and use one attempt.

`npm test` in the existing Hashgraph SDK bridge passed **62/62**, zero skips. New SDK protobuf round-trip tests prove exact allowance units `9007199254740993`, token/owner/spender identity, contract ABI, fees and transaction IDs, plus rejection of ambiguous/out-of-range amounts. This is construction evidence only: fungible signed execution, C# configuration/provider wiring, and funded live custody/release are still incomplete. The old C# fungible lock/unlock path must not be represented as implemented escrow. No deployment or merge has occurred at this checkpoint.

Provider-backed public manager APIs use one of two intentional boundaries:

1. Avatar, AvatarDetail, Holon, Search and Wallet persistence enter `OASISHyperDrive` through a typed request when `HyperDriveMode == OASISHyperDrive2`.
2. Feature managers persist through an injected `HolonManager`/`AvatarManager`; they do not select or mutate a provider themselves and therefore inherit boundary 1.

An explicit-provider request is request-scoped. It calls the selected registered provider directly through the router and does not change `ProviderManager.CurrentStorageProvider`. Automatic failover and balancing apply only to non-explicit routes. The dedicated Edge/hosted synchronization API is a separate transactional boundary: its provider transaction writes the canonical mutation, change feed and fan-out record atomically.

## Public operation matrix

The human-readable surface matrix below is backed by the exhaustive checked-in
[`HYPERDRIVE_V2_PUBLIC_MANAGER_METHOD_MANIFEST.csv`](HYPERDRIVE_V2_PUBLIC_MANAGER_METHOD_MANIFEST.csv).
It contains all **815** public method declarations under `API.Core/Managers`, including
overloads, and classifies **655** provider-backed/delegating methods by their intentional
boundary. `scripts/verify_hyperdrive_provider_io_manifest.ps1` reparses the source and fails
when a method is added, removed, or has its signature changed without a manifest review.

The WEB4 HTTP surface is independently catalogued in
[`HYPERDRIVE_WEB4_ROUTE_MANIFEST.csv`](HYPERDRIVE_WEB4_ROUTE_MANIFEST.csv). It currently
contains all **691** `[HttpGet|Post|Put|Patch|Delete]` controller actions and records whether
each action enters a mode-aware manager, delegates to another reviewed action/manager,
operates the HyperDrive/provider control plane, targets an explicit external/provider-native
boundary, or is local/non-storage. `Scripts/verify_web4_hyperdrive_route_manifest.ps1`
reparses the controller source and fails when the action surface drifts. This closes the
route-to-manager inventory gap without pretending that a Swagger request made with `{}` is
an end-to-end business assertion.

| Public API surface | Provider-backed operations | V2 path | Sync/async and explicit-provider disposition | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AvatarManager` | load by ID/username/email/keys/tokens, load all, save, delete, AvatarDetail, karma, inventory | Direct typed `StorageOperationRequest`/avatar requests | Sync and async route through their corresponding V2 helpers; explicit provider remains request-scoped | `Managers/AvatarManager/AvatarManager-Load*.cs`, `AvatarManager-Save*.cs`, `AvatarManager-Delete*.cs`, `AvatarManager-Karma.cs`, `AvatarManager-Inventory.cs` |
| `HolonManager` | load by ID/provider key, load all, metadata queries, parent/child traversal, save, delete, settings | Direct typed `StorageOperationRequest` | Sync and async parity is exercised by provider-execution tests; explicit provider does not use automatic failover | `Managers/HolonManager/*.cs` |
| `SearchManager` | search holons/avatars | Direct typed search operation | Provider argument is carried in the request; empty result sets are terminal, successful results | `Managers/SearchManager.cs` |
| `WalletManager` | provider-wallet persistence stored with avatars plus provider-native wallet/token calls | Avatar persistence uses the V2 boundary; native chain calls deliberately execute on the explicitly requested wallet provider | Explicit wallet-provider selection is part of the wallet contract and does not change global storage state | `Managers/WalletManager*.cs` |
| `KeyManager` | avatar keys and wallet key material | Delegates avatar/provider-wallet storage to injected Avatar/Wallet managers | Both ID and username overloads use the injected runtime | `Managers/KeyManager*.cs` |
| `MessagingManager` | canonical messages and read state | Delegates to injected HolonManager | Async-only durable feature API; provider failures remain errors | `Managers/MessagingManager.cs` |
| `ChatManager` | sessions, messages, chat statistics | Delegates to injected HolonManager | Async-only feature API | `Managers/ChatManager.cs` |
| `CompetitionManager` | leaderboards, tournaments, enrollment | Delegates to injected HolonManager settings | Async-only feature API; load-before-create accepts authoritative not-found | `Managers/CompetitionManager.cs` |
| `EggsManager` | egg inventory and lifecycle | Delegates to injected HolonManager settings | Async-only feature API | `Managers/EggsManager.cs` |
| `GiftsManager` | canonical gifts and lifecycle/history | Delegates to injected HolonManager | Async-only feature API | `Managers/GiftsManager.cs` |
| `FilesManager` | canonical file metadata | Delegates to injected HolonManager; binary transport remains the selected file provider's responsibility | Async feature API | `Managers/FilesManager.cs` |
| `SeedsManager` | SEEDS records and state | Delegates to injected HolonManager or explicitly selected SEEDS provider for provider-native operations | Explicit provider-native calls are not storage load balancing | `Managers/SeedsManager*.cs` |
| `ClanManager`, `MissionManager`, `QuestManager`, `MapManager`, `GeoNFTManager`, `NFTManager` | domain holons and NFT/geo records | Delegate to Holon/Avatar managers for storage; blockchain/NFT execution is explicitly provider-targeted | Storage inherits V2; chain-side actions intentionally do not fail over as generic storage operations | corresponding manager sources |
| `ProviderManager` | registration, activation, health and routing policy | Local runtime control plane, not persisted domain data | Global mutation is limited to administrator configuration/boot, never a routed request | `Managers/OASIS HyperDrive/Provider Management/ProviderManager*.cs` |
| HyperDrive sync managers | outbox exchange, checkpoint, commands, change feed, fan-out | Dedicated transactional synchronization contracts | Provider transaction owns atomic enrollment; dispatcher owns leases, bounded retry and ordered completion | `Managers/OASIS HyperDrive/Synchronization/*.cs`; Mongo `MongoDBOASIS.HyperDriveSync.cs` |
| Analytics/AI/bridge orchestration | metrics, recommendations, chain bridge calls | Local analytics state or explicitly targeted external provider | Deliberately outside generic storage routing | `Managers/OASIS HyperDrive/*.cs`, `Managers/Bridge/*.cs` |

## Not-found and failure semantics

- A null `OASISResult` wrapper is a provider failure and is eligible for automatic failover.
- `IsError == true` is a provider failure and is eligible for automatic failover.
- A non-error result whose payload is null is the existing authoritative **not found** response and is terminal. This is required for safe load-before-create workflows.
- A non-error empty collection is an authoritative empty query and is terminal.
- Explicit-provider calls never silently fail over.

These rules are executable in `HyperDriveProviderExecutionTests`, including provider-error recovery, disabled failover, explicit-provider behavior, authoritative null payload, and empty collection behavior.

## Replication boundaries

- Ordinary WEB4 V2 mutations replicate inline. `EnableHostedSync` no longer causes those operations to claim false durable deferral.
- Edge synchronization mutations use `IHostedHyperDriveProvider.ApplyOperationsAsync`. MongoDB commits the entity mutation, change feed, device sequence/terminal operation and fan-out record in one transaction.
- `HostedHyperDriveFanOutDispatcher` provides exclusive leases, lease renewal, ordered batch handling and bounded retry/backoff. Failed work remains durable for a later worker/restart.
- Replica failures are warnings with structured attempt diagnostics; they never turn a successful primary mutation into a false primary failure.

## Static audit procedure

The matrix was checked by searching `Managers/**/*.cs` for direct provider method calls and classifying every result as:

- central V2 request routing;
- an explicit provider-native action;
- a feature-manager delegation to Avatar/Holon/Wallet;
- or local, non-provider state.

The former duplicate `ProviderManagerNew` and `ProviderConfigurator` implementation had no production references and was removed. `ProviderManager` is now the sole runtime policy owner.

Repeatable gate:

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify_hyperdrive_provider_io_manifest.ps1
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify_web4_hyperdrive_route_manifest.ps1
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify_hyperdrive_v2_operation_contracts.ps1
```

Verified result on 2026-10-07: `815 public manager methods; 655 provider-backed/delegating methods intentionally classified`.
Verified WEB4 result on 2026-10-07: `691 controller actions; 597 non-local actions intentionally classified`.
Verified V2 router result on 2026-10-07: all `42` storage operations have synchronous/asynchronous handler parity and an executable provider-boundary contract.

## Executable evidence

- `HyperDriveProviderExecutionTests`: includes direct config-flag-to-routing and live-latency-to-next-selection contract tests.
- HyperDrive-filtered Core contract run on 2026-10-07: 298/298 passing, zero skipped. This includes disposable-provider assertions for Legacy failover, replication and load-balancing as characterization only, plus executable provider-boundary coverage for every V2 storage operation.
- Complete Core unit-test assembly on 2026-10-07: 309/309 passing, zero skipped; TRX captured locally at `artifacts/hyperdrive-dual-mode/hyperdrive-dual-mode-full.trx`.
- Hosted sync coordinator and fan-out subset: 16/16 passing.
- Real isolated three-member MongoDB replica-set evidence: 42/42 transaction/replay/retry tests plus 1/1 abrupt-primary-termination/idempotency test. The repeatable runner is `scripts/run_hosted_mongo_release_evidence.ps1`; TRX output is written beneath the selected artifacts directory.
- WEB4 Release build: succeeded with zero errors (866 pre-existing warnings reported by the compiler).

### Four-provider real-runtime matrix

`Scripts/run_four_provider_hyperdrive_matrix.ps1` is the repeatable first provider matrix. It refuses to attach to pre-existing loopback services and owns and cleans up every runtime it starts:

| Provider | Isolated runtime | Executed evidence |
|---|---|---|
| MongoDBOASIS | Portable three-member MongoDB replica set | 43/43 transaction, replay, retry, election and abrupt-primary-termination tests |
| SQLLiteDBOASIS | Per-test temporary SQLite database | 3/3 activation and avatar/holon persistence round trips |
| IPFSOASIS | Pinned Kubo 0.43.1 daemon, offline and loopback-only | 1/1 activation and holon save/load round trip |
| EthereumOASIS | Ganache chain 31337 with deterministic development-only accounts | 3/3 contract deployment, activation validation, holon save/load and wrong-chain rejection |

The Ethereum run uses only free prefunded currency on a disposable local development chain. It never contacts Ethereum mainnet and cannot spend real gas. Evidence from the 2026-10-07 run is under `artifacts/four-provider-matrix`; `summary.json` records PASS and the TRX files retain individual assertions.

This matrix exposed two false-positive activation defects. IPFS previously treated construction of an HTTP client as successful activation without contacting a daemon. Ethereum did the same without validating the RPC endpoint, chain ID or contract bytecode, and its reflective Nethereum constructor lookup was incompatible with Nethereum 7. Both providers now fail activation unless the external system is actually reachable and correctly configured, allowing V2 failover to operate on truthful provider state.

The live credentialed run passed 2/2 tests with zero skips: missing credentials are rejected truthfully, while authenticated activation, holon upload, gateway readback, identity verification and unpin cleanup all succeeded. JWT/API-key authentication is propagated consistently to CRUD calls. |
| EOSIOOASIS / TelosOASIS | PASS | Official Antelope Spring 1.2.2 `nodeos`/`keosd` and Antelope CDT 4.1.1 on loopback; the checked-in OASIS contract was compiled and deployed, then EOSIOOASIS passed 2/2 and TelosOASIS passed 2/2 signed avatar/holon create, read, update and hard-delete tests with zero skips. Telos storage uses the same real Antelope client and contract rather than fabricating unsigned `push_transaction` payloads. A .NET 10-compatible HTTP handler replaces eos-sharp 2.2.0's removed BinaryFormatter cache-key implementation without bypassing signing or node I/O. |
| AzureCosmosDBOASIS | PASS | Uses the official `Microsoft.Azure.Cosmos` SDK against Microsoft's Azure Cosmos DB Linux vNext emulator over HTTPS in gateway mode. 2/2 activation, avatar create/read/update/query/delete and holon create/read/delete tests passed with zero skips. Activation creates/verifies the configured database and `/id`-partitioned containers, and loopback-only emulator TLS handling does not weaken certificate validation for real Azure endpoints. |
| AWSOASIS | PASS | Consolidated onto the official `AWSSDK.DynamoDBv2` implementation. `Scripts/run_aws_provider_evidence.ps1` checksum-verifies and starts Amazon's official DynamoDB Local runtime, then proves 2/2 activation, avatar CRUD, holon CRUD and search tests with zero skips. The former fabricated unsigned `/dynamodb/...` HTTP prototype and its backup were deleted rather than merely excluded from compilation. |
| GoogleCloudOASIS | PASS (Firestore storage path) | Uses the official `Google.Cloud.Firestore` SDK against Google's Firestore emulator; 2/2 truthful activation-probe, avatar CRUD and holon CRUD tests passed with zero skips. Activation now performs a reversible write/delete instead of accepting client construction. Cloud Storage and BigQuery are optional auxiliary services and were disabled for this isolated storage-provider run. |
| ThreeFoldOASIS | PASS | Replaced the fabricated custom `/avatars` and `/holons` HTTP contract with ThreeFold QSS S3-CAS through the official `AWSSDK.S3` client. The official QSS server was built from `threefoldtech/qss_s3_cas` and run locally; 2/2 activation, bucket provisioning, avatar CRUD and holon CRUD tests passed with zero skips. Activation uses portable S3 `ListBuckets`/`PutBucket` operations because QSS intentionally does not implement `GetBucketAcl`. |
| HoloOASIS | PASS (1/1) | Official Holochain 0.7.0 conductor plus the HDK 0.7/HDI 0.8 OASIS hApp. The integration test performs Avatar and Holon create/read/update/delete through HoloNET; delete uses the asynchronous ORM call so the websocket callback cannot deadlock behind the sync wrapper. Direct Holo hosting without a provisioned app client now fails activation explicitly instead of reporting placeholder success. |
| AptosOASIS | REVERIFY | Uses Aptos Labs' `Aptos` .NET SDK 0.1.1-beta for BCS construction, Ed25519 signing, submission, confirmation and view calls. Holons, avatars and avatar details use the deployed Move table with enumeration, lookup, deletion, filtering, search, export and proximity operations. Native APT balance/transfer use SDK view and signed entry-function calls with exact octa conversion. OASIS custom-token mint/transfer/balance/lock/unlock/burn and NFT mint/load/send/lock/unlock/burn persist through signed, confirmed Move contract transactions. Bridge transfers validate sender ownership, return real transaction hashes and resolve committed status through the Aptos REST API; generic entry-function execution is SDK-signed and accepts both `module::function` and fully-qualified identifiers. The former Solana wallet dependency, unsigned JSON storage/token/NFT submission, fabricated parsing/signing fallbacks, nonexistent Move functions and excluded legacy files were removed. `Scripts/run_aptos_provider_evidence.ps1` checksum-verifies and reuses official Aptos CLI 9.6.0 at a stable path, binds the node to loopback, runs non-interactively with a deterministic disposable key, runs Move tests (1/1), deploys/initializes the module, and passes SDK activation, storage/query/avatar, native balance/transfer, custom-token lifecycle, NFT lifecycle, bridge/status, generic entry-function and unreachable-node tests (10/10, zero skips). No public APT or paid gas is used. |
| ArweaveOASIS | PASS | Transaction creation, deep hashing, RSA-PSS signing, posting, data reads, network probing and GraphQL tag queries now run through ArweaveTeam's official `arweave-js` 2.1.0 SDK; the incorrect hand-written signer and synthetic activation were removed. The SDK bridge uses JSON over redirected stdin/stdout, never command-line wallet secrets, and the provider activates only after a real network probe and writable JWK validation. Permanent-data deletes are signed tombstone transactions and all ID/email/username/list reads suppress tombstoned avatars; holon ID reads do the same. `Scripts/run_arweave_provider_evidence.ps1` restores integrity-locked npm dependencies, owns an isolated ArLocal 1.1.66 protocol runtime, creates and funds a disposable SDK wallet, and passes 6/6 signed avatar CRUD/query, holon CRUD, enumeration, tombstone and unreachable-gateway tests with zero skips. No public AR or paid storage is used. |
| NEAROASIS | REVERIFY | Uses the official `near-api-js` 7.3.1 SDK for network/code probes, Ed25519 keys, account state, signed transfers, transaction finality, contract views and contract calls. The checked-in `near-sdk-js` 2.0.0 contract is freshly compiled and deployed for every run; initialization fixes an explicit contract owner, storage mutations are owner-only, NFT mutations enforce token ownership, and custom-token transfers enforce source-account authorization. Avatar, avatar-detail and holon CRUD, provider-key lookup, parent/recursive trees, metadata filtering, search, import/export and hard/soft deletion persist in contract state through the shared hardened OASIS serializer. Native NEAR transfers, OASIS custom-token and NFT lifecycles, native/NFT bridge paths and provider-originated wallet history are real finalized chain operations; history is an OASIS contract audit index because NEAR JSON-RPC does not offer account-history enumeration without a separate Lake/indexer service. `Scripts/run_near_provider_evidence.ps1` restores integrity-locked official SDK dependencies, compiles on WSL's native filesystem with an isolated LLVM temp path, owns a disposable NEAR sandbox, deploys and initializes the contract, and passes 12/12 activation, storage/query, account/key, native transfer/history, custom-token, NFT and bridge tests with zero skips. No public NEAR or paid gas is used. |

Both EVM providers now verify RPC reachability, chain identity and deployed bytecode before reporting active. The standalone Arbitrum provider has the same checks. Web3 mutations wait for successful mined receipts; holons use the correct contract collection; tuple results use Nethereum's typed struct/output mapping; and create/update/delete now mutate actual chain state. This prevents HyperDrive V2 from selecting a provider that only constructed a local client successfully or reporting success before persistence. `Web3CoreOASIS/package.json` no longer attempts to install Node as a project dependency, the Hardhat configuration no longer requires a production private key for local compilation, and all 148 Solidity sources compile on a clean dependency install.

The portable provider runner also validates cached ZIP archives before reuse and re-expands empty/corrupt runtime directories. This prevents an interrupted download from poisoning every later verification run.

These statuses are deliberately not rolled up as “all providers work.” V2 routing/failover/load-balancing/replication contracts are complete at the HyperDrive boundary, but a provider can participate only when its own activation and operations are truthful. Each row is promoted only after its listed real SDK/runtime evidence passes.

Moralis is intentionally excluded from the HyperDrive storage-provider matrix. Moralis is a read-only Web3 indexing and enrichment API, not a mutable persistence service; the repository's own `Moralis-vs-OASIS-Comparison.md` documents that boundary. The boot loader previously contained a dead `MoralisOASIS` switch arm whose construction and result assignment were commented out, so selecting it returned no provider while the coverage gate incorrectly counted it as registered storage. That dead arm and its unused storage-error callback were removed. Moralis remains a provider type for its genuine query/enrichment surface, but it is not advertised or selectable as a HyperDrive failover, replication, or load-balancing target. Fabricating avatar/holon CRUD on top of its read-only API would make activation and durability claims false.

ActivityPub is likewise excluded from generic HyperDrive storage selection. It is a federated social protocol: Mastodon-compatible account and status endpoints represent remote actors and posts, not an OASIS avatar/holon database. The former boot-loader registration supplied no OAuth token and nevertheless advertised the adapter as a failover/replication target, where writes could not succeed and account creation/deletion would be semantically unsafe. The storage registration and unused storage-error callback were removed; ActivityPub remains available as a social/network integration and must be exercised through protocol-specific authenticated operations rather than generic storage routing.

Bitcoin is not a generic document store and is excluded from HyperDrive storage routing. The previous implementation attempted to serialize an entire avatar or holon into one `OP_RETURN` output and used obsolete or mismatched RPC/indexer calls to read it back. Standard Bitcoin relay policy only permits a small data-carrier payload, so realistic OASIS records cannot round-trip that way; silently adding an unrelated side database would not constitute Bitcoin-backed persistence. The boot-loader registration and `Storage`/`NFT`/`SmartContract` capability claims were removed. Bitcoin remains categorized as a blockchain provider for real wallet, transaction, and hash-anchoring work, while full records must be persisted by an actual storage provider and may separately anchor their content hash to Bitcoin.

### Cosmos implementation and verification checkpoint

Cosmos is **not yet a passing complete provider**. Its replacement storage backend uses the shared key-value provider implementation and official CosmJS 0.39.0 clients. Four Rust unit tests pass for storage authorization/pagination, token supply/balances/locks/burns and NFT ownership/locks/burns. The WebAssembly compatibility blocker was resolved on 2026-10-11: disabling features only for the contract left bulk-memory instructions in the precompiled Rust standard library. Rebuilding the standard library and compiler builtins with the same disabled features produced bytecode accepted by the existing wasmd VM.

`Scripts/TestHosts/build-cosmos-contract.sh` pins the installed Rust 1.99.0 compiler, uses its matching `rust-src` component, and reuses `/var/tmp/oasis-cosmos-contract-target` with an exclusive build lock, offline locked dependencies and two workers. It disables bulk-memory and bulk-memory-opt along with unsupported related features, using `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1` and `-Z build-std=std,panic_abort`. These are unstable compiler interfaces, so changing the compiler requires actual VM deployment validation rather than assuming compatibility. The matching source component occupies approximately 83 MiB; no second compiler/runtime was installed. The target cache measured about 900 MiB after this build. Re-running the script reused cached output successfully.

The live CosmJS contract test passed **1/1, zero skips** against the existing ledger. It covers actual upload/instantiation, signed storage writes, transaction readback, pagination, a funded unauthorized writer rejected by the contract, wrong-chain rejection and deletion. The deployment fixture is retained beside the compiled Wasm. This closes the VM compatibility and direct SDK storage deployment gate, not .NET lifecycle or V2 routing verification.

The provider's NFT and custom-token interfaces now call signed CosmJS contract execution instead of producing unsigned transaction-shaped JSON. The contract enforces storage-owner mint authority, token issuer locks and actual NFT holder transfer/burn authority. These are OASIS custom assets, not advertised CW20/CW721 implementations. Custom tokens have eight decimal places; precision loss and Uint128 overflow are rejected. NFT identifiers use the canonical `<contract-address>#<token-id>` form. Mint currently accepts one unique NFT per call and rejects incompatible ERC/SPL standard selections. Native token transfers use the actual configured bank denomination. Generic query, execute, upload and instantiate methods call the official SDK and require real committed receipts.

The complete Cosmos .NET integration test project passed **11/11, zero skips**, on 2026-10-11 against this same deployed contract and ledger; the retained report is `TestResults/CosmosRuntime/cosmos-live.trx`. Its two storage tests cover avatar/detail persistence, lookup indexes, versions, holon metadata/parent indexes and deletion. Its three asset tests cover custom-token supply/balance/transfer/lock/burn, NFT mint/read/transfer/lock/escrow/release/burn with ownership enforcement, and generic signed contract execution/query. The six native/failure tests cover transfers, transaction status and balance changes, sender/chain rejection, exact amount validation, wallet recovery, bridge pool balances and unreachable-RPC activation failure. This establishes the tested direct-provider paths, not every request option or Cosmos V2 orchestration. The SDK bridge's three wallet/key/RPC tests passed with zero skips after these changes. Provider disposal prevents subsequent SDK subprocess operations.

The reusable local blockchain is official `cosmwasm/wasmd:v0.70.3`, pinned to image digest `sha256:3741178a4d747fd5cf281550ab4ac30d771163af1c543f9da503c411a1691773`, in the existing root-Podman WSL container `oasis-wasmd`. Its RPC binds to host loopback port 26657 and its chain ID is `oasis-cosmos-local`. The older `oasis-cosmos` container is Azure Cosmos DB, not this blockchain. Only public development keys and locally minted `stake` are used.

`CosmosTransferTests` and `CosmosFailureTests` passed **6/6, zero skips**, on 2026-10-08. The live chain assertions cover SDK-signed .NET transfers with committed transaction status and exact recipient balance deltas; decoded sender/recipient transaction history; real native bridge deposits and sender-signed withdrawals including gas debit; mnemonic recovery matching compressed secp256k1 public keys; wrong-sender and wrong-chain rejection; exact amount validation; and unreachable-RPC activation failure. The fixed report is `TestResults/CosmosRuntime/cosmos-transfers.trx`. These tests do not substitute for contract-storage or all-provider orchestration evidence.

The boot loader now passes the actual chain ID and signing key to the correct constructor arguments. DNA exposes `ContractAddress`, `AddressPrefix`, `GasPrice`, `NativeDenom` and `NativeDecimals`; native transfers and balances no longer infer an asset from a fee denomination or sum unrelated coins. Native wallet operations do not require a storage contract. Avatar-addressed transfers load the existing WalletManager default wallets, request the sender's private key only, and use the same signed transfer implementation. Those WalletManager-backed routes still require their own integration evidence.

The Cosmos explicit-amount burn update passed its focused live test on 2026-10-11: `TestResults/CosmosRuntime/cosmos-explicit-burn.trx` contains 1/1 passing test with zero skips. Burning 0.25 from a 1.25-token recipient balance leaves exactly one token and reduces supply to 12.25; subsequent explicit burns reconcile supply and balances to zero. The provider converts the shared `Amount` to exact eight-decimal Uint128 units. The contract rejects zero and insufficient-balance burns, subtracts only those units and preserves the remainder; all four Rust tests passed, including partial-burn and supply assertions. The existing CosmJS storage/authorization test passed 1/1 against newly deployed matching bytecode on the same retained wasmd ledger. The full direct-provider suite was rerun against that bytecode and passed 11/11, zero skips, in 4 minutes 14 seconds; the Cosmos V2 routing filter passed 2/2, zero skips, in 41 seconds. Their fixed reports are `TestResults/CosmosRuntime/cosmos-live.trx` and `TestResults/HyperDriveCosmos/cosmos-v2-routing.trx`. The verified deployment is code ID 2, SHA-256 `E2BEB864D6F9C0CCBF85254E3533AD7CAFC8CBFB029C847695687D544ECB3C7F`, at `wasm1nc5tatafv6eyq7llkr2gv50ff9e22mnf70qgjlv737ktmt4eswrqr5j2ht` on `oasis-cosmos-local`.

Both reruns reused the retained ledger and installed source SDK through `OASIS_COSMOS_SDK_BRIDGE`; no runtime, dependency installation or second environment was created. The node and container were stopped afterward, retaining the ledger and compact reports. The updated contract requires the burn `amount` field; old whole-balance payloads are no longer valid. HTTP mapping, packaged SDK execution, wallet-backed API routes and wider operation/failure matrices remain gates. The two V2 cases prove owned SDK-transport failover, actual replication and selection from measured real-store reads, not a node outage or all provider combinations.

### Sui implementation and verification checkpoint

Sui remains **outside the passing-provider set**. The provider now uses pinned official `@mysten/sui` 2.35.0 for Ed25519 wallets and BIP39 account derivation, with pinned `@scure/bip39` 2.4.0. Private keys use the SDK's canonical `suiprivkey` encoding; account recovery uses the Sui derivation path rather than hashing arbitrary text into a seed. Five SDK tests and four .NET wallet/validation tests passed with zero skips on 2026-10-08. The fixed .NET report is `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-sdk-wallets.trx`. The provider and boot-loader builds succeeded with zero errors; the boot-loader retains 866 warnings.

DNA and boot-loader registration now separate `PrivateKey` from `ChainId`; the constructor no longer interprets a network/chain label as signing credentials. `ChainId`, when supplied, must match the SDK's genesis chain identifier, not the human-readable network name. The native address-to-address transfer method now creates and signs a programmable transaction using the official gRPC client, validates sender ownership and requires a successful transaction digest. Amounts must fit u64 MIST exactly; zero, negatives, precision loss and overflow fail explicitly. The native transfer path currently rejects nonempty memo text because it has no memo field; it does not silently discard the caller's text. Balance errors no longer become successful zero balances.

Native transfers now have real consensus evidence. The official SDK runtime test passed 1/1 and the .NET `SuiNativeTransferTests` passed 1/1, both with zero skips, on 2026-10-08. Assertions verify signed execution, exact recipient balance deltas, actual receipt status, wrong-signer rejection and wrong-chain errors rather than successful zero balances. The SDK test reconciles the sender balance against actual computation/storage costs minus storage rebate; a positive-fee-only assumption is invalid when Sui gas smashing rebates deleted coins. The fixed .NET report is `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-native-transfers.trx`. The SDK test also passed after a controlled stop/restart with the same genesis blob, without regenerating the ledger.

The approved official [Sui mainnet-v1.81.1 release](https://github.com/MystenLabs/sui/releases/tag/mainnet-v1.81.1) is installed once at `/var/tmp/oasis-runtimes/sui-1.81.1/sui` in the existing WSL environment. The Linux archive SHA-256 matched `d921910fdedf5ae2bc3f08a7236c57819a457ce53a8cc60c85f3653c7831b5d4`. Only the 208 MiB executable was retained; the 1.02 GiB archive was deleted. `Scripts/TestHosts/install-sui-runtime.sh` checks for/reuses the pinned executable, verifies downloaded content and extracts only `sui`. `start-sui-test-network.sh` locks and reuses `/var/tmp/oasis-sui/network`, creates a one-validator local genesis only when absent and never force-regenerates it. Local faucet funding uses no public-chain assets. The node was stopped after verification; the retained reusable ledger was approximately 31 MiB.

The replacement Move storage contract in `contracts/sources/storage.move` stores complete serialized values in a shared table and authorizes mutations against its initialized owner. Its two Move tests passed. The live `SdkBridge/storage-runtime.test.mjs` passed 1/1 with zero skips: signed publication, 105 complete Unicode records across pagination, exact readback, update, unauthorized-signer rejection, deletion and idempotent removal. A `finally` block removes the test records. The fixed ignored deployment cache retains the synthetic local signer and published object IDs so subsequent runs reuse the same contract when bytecode and genesis match. No public-chain transactions are used.

`install-sui-framework.sh` retains only 1.8 MiB of framework and standard-library sources at the runtime's pinned source revision, without cloning the repository or retaining a source archive. `build-sui-contract.sh` reuses those dependencies and the existing local client configuration. Its `mainnet` build-environment label selects the framework's system package addresses for compilation; unit tests execute in-process and publication uses only the explicitly configured local RPC. The SDK storage operations validate the object's package ABI, decode canonical BCS, sign mutations, wait for successful execution and reject non-advancing pagination. Only a typed object-not-found error becomes a missing value; other errors propagate.

The .NET provider now inherits the shared key-value storage implementation; 68 old activation/CRUD/query overrides and unused lossy parsers were removed. `SuiStorageBackend` uses the same `SuiSdkClient` transport as wallet operations. Activation verifies a real owner-signed put/read/delete rather than an HTTP-page response. DNA and boot-loader registration expose `StorageObjectId` alongside the published package's `ContractAddress`. Disposal closes the SDK transport and clears activation state; geographic queries filter persisted `Latitude`/`Longitude` metadata instead of reporting all avatars as nearby.

The real .NET storage/native suite passed 4/4 with zero skips on 2026-10-08, using the retained local deployment: avatar/detail roundtrip, username/email indexes and prior versions; holon metadata, parent indexes and deletion; provider-key lookup, recursive loading, import/export, text search and proximity filtering; and signed native transfer/status checks. Fixture records are removed in `finally` blocks. The fixed report is `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-storage-and-native.trx`. Wallet tests passed again 4/4 with zero skips. The boot-loader build succeeded with zero errors and 351 warnings in this incremental build. No new runtime, ledger or SDK environment was allocated, and the node was stopped after the run.

Sui NFT mint/read/transfer/burn now use actual address-owned Move objects in `contracts/sources/nft.move`, official SDK signing and VM ownership enforcement. The SDK lifecycle test passed 1/1 with zero skips: two distinct minted objects, full Unicode metadata, ownership transfer, previous-owner burn rejection and deletion. Three Move tests passed. The .NET lifecycle test passed 1/1, and the combined storage/native/NFT suite passed 5/5 with zero skips; its fixed report is `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-storage-native-nft.trx`. Owned test NFTs are burned in `finally` blocks. The same pinned runtime and ledger were reused; a new package revision was published because its bytecode changed, without regenerating genesis.

Batch mint responses retain `Web3NFT` for existing callers and expose all minted objects through the additive `Web3NFTs` property on `IWeb3NFTTransactionResponse`. Each minted OASIS record has a distinct persisted ID. The API Core change is pinned to `f6fdca9c9bc248f2f7c54b1b6cce80eace5e82aa` in both the parent gitlink and Railway manifest. The SDK transport explicitly encodes stdin/stdout/stderr as UTF-8: the first .NET NFT test exposed Windows console-code-page corruption of an emoji; the corrected transport passed exact Unicode roundtrip assertions. Mint recipient resolution reuses WalletManager ID/username/email lookups, but those avatar-addressed paths still require integration tests. Other NFT standards and nonempty transaction memos fail explicitly rather than being silently reinterpreted.

The dependency audit also found an existing HoloNET-ORM manifest mismatch. Its checkout and parent gitlink both contain `8850384c63af2b0722f4a73c3e8a0584427a6a7f` (record-payload and complex-field mapping), while Railway's manifest still specified the preceding `24861168b3b95d139947999a343e17890d953c5f`. The manifest now pins the actual local/parent revision; the ORM checkout was not replaced or reset.

The new token contract in `contracts/sources/token.move` creates standard Sui `Coin<TOKEN>` assets through the current coin registry API, stores its treasury capability in a shared issuer-controlled currency object, and provides holder-authorized burns. Locking moves the holder's spendable coins into address-owned `LockedCoin` escrow objects; only their owner can unlock them, and escrow objects cannot be publicly transferred outside the module. All seven Move tests passed, including issuer and unlock authorization failures and exact supply/value accounting. The compiler reports one storage-efficiency lint because escrow retains a `Coin` object instead of only its `Balance`; this is not a skipped test.

The official SDK now signs and waits for token mint, transfer, full-balance burn, lock and unlock operations, paginates coin/escrow inputs, reads actual supply and locked/spendable balances, and completes the coin registry's required post-publication registration transaction. The real `SdkBridge/token-runtime.test.mjs` passed 1/1 with zero skips, including registered symbol/decimals, issuer rejection, receipt status, transfer balance changes, locked funds becoming unspendable, unlock restoring them, holder burn and cleanup to the pre-run supply. The fixture caches its synthetic recipient key in the ignored fixed evidence directory and reuses it across runs. The initial failed SDK run revealed a response-shape error in the new token balance path; it now reads the SDK's documented total-balance field. That failed run left 400 synthetic token units at an ephemeral recipient whose key was not retained; the persistent fixture prevents recurrence. The retained local test ledger is disposable and has not been regenerated to hide the residue. Storage and NFT SDK runtime tests passed again 1/1 each; wallet SDK tests passed 5/5.

DNA, registration and the single SDK transport expose `CurrencyObjectId`. The boot-loader build passed with zero errors and 1,310 dependency warnings. All ten unsigned .NET token entry points were replaced by the same official SDK operations. Requests require the configured canonical coin type and exact positive amounts with at most nine decimal places; mismatched public keys, conflicting key/seed credentials, unsupported memos and unavailable avatar wallets fail explicitly. Burn now requires the explicit shared request Amount, converts it to exact u64 units and burns a split Coin while retaining the remaining spendable balance. Lock still consumes the signer's full spendable balance because its request interface exposes no amount. Unlock restores that signer's owned escrows. Native decimal balance reads also use the official SDK and propagate network/chain errors rather than reporting a successful zero.

The updated Sui .NET token lifecycle passed 1/1 with zero skips on 2026-10-11 in `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-explicit-token-burn.trx`, including a 0.25-token burn from a one-token holder balance and independent readback of the remaining 0.75. Cleanup burns only the authoritative balance read from the retained synthetic fixture. The focused validation test passed 1/1 in `sui-burn-validation.trx`, asserting amount-specific errors for zero, negative, excess precision and decimal overflow before signing or network access. These direct-provider results do not prove the HTTP burn route or other providers' explicit-amount handling.

The .NET token lifecycle test passed 1/1 with zero skips, and the combined storage/native/NFT/token suite passed 6/6 with zero skips on 2026-10-08. Its fixed report is `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-storage-native-nft-token.trx`. Assertions include issuer rejection, committed receipts, exact transfer balances, locked funds becoming unspendable, unlock/burn cleanup, incorrect asset types, excess precision, wrong signing credentials and insufficient funds. The native test additionally verifies decimal balance parity and wrong-chain errors. The same installed runtime, SDK environment and retained ledger were reused; the local node was stopped after verification to bound disk growth.

NFT lock/unlock now uses address-owned `LockedNFT` escrow objects with no `store` ability, replacing the old transfer-to-package-address implementation. The escrow preserves the original NFT object ID, creator and metadata; unlocking requires its owner. Both .NET requests validate a supplied OASIS NFT GUID against the persisted metadata instead of treating that GUID as a chain object ID. The official SDK paginates the signer's escrow objects and executes the actual Move lock/unlock calls. Wrapped NFTs cannot be spent or read directly through their original object ID while locked; `nftEscrowGet` reads their metadata through the owner's escrow. All nine Move tests passed, and the SDK NFT lifecycle and .NET NFT escrow lifecycle each passed 1/1 with zero skips. The fixed .NET report is `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-nft-escrow.trx`. Fixtures track owned escrows and unlock them before burn cleanup if interrupted by a later assertion. Contract publication must finish before dependent tests consume the deployment cache; an initial concurrent run used the prior package and failed with a missing lock function, then passed against the completed deployment.

Native bridge deposits and withdrawals now execute real signed SUI transfers between the caller and the custody wallet derived from the configured provider signing key. A package ID is not a wallet, so it is no longer used as a destination. Missing custody credentials, wrong sender keys, excess precision and insufficient funds return actual errors; success returns a committed digest with `Completed` status. These existing interface methods implement custodial settlement, not a trustless cross-chain proof protocol. The bridge coordinator remains responsible for authorized cross-chain release and rollback policy.

NFT settlement transfers the original object into and out of that custody wallet rather than minting a replacement or treating an OASIS GUID as an object ID. `nftTokenAddress` is the configured collection package ID and `tokenId` is the actual NFT object ID. An optional source digest must be a successful Sui transaction that transferred this exact existing NFT into custody from another address. Its output version must still match the current object, and the release transaction consumes that exact object reference, binding validation to execution. Unrelated receipts, released-object replay and stale receipts after a second withdrawal fail. A foreign-chain digest cannot be verified by a Sui node; the coordinator must validate its own source-chain proof instead of passing it as a Sui receipt. The .NET `SuiBridgeTests` passed 1/1 with zero skips, proving exact native recipient balance changes, committed status, credential/precision/funds failures, original NFT identity, custody ownership and receipt replay rejection. The fixed report is `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-bridge.trx`.

The combined .NET storage/native/NFT/token/bridge suite passed 7/7 with zero skips on 2026-10-08. Its fixed report is `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-storage-native-nft-token-bridge.trx`. This verifies the real provider paths together against the same retained local deployment and pinned runtime; it does not prove all public API routes or multi-provider V2 orchestration.

Wallet history now uses the official SDK's gRPC ledger service with an OR filter for sender and affected address, rather than the obsolete `sui_getTransactions` JSON-RPC request. Reads cover incoming and outgoing receipts through the node's captured checkpoint height, continue item-limited and scan-limited pages using authoritative watermarks, and reject duplicate digests or non-advancing cursors. Complete-history requests fail explicitly on pruned ledgers, missing bounds, incomplete receipts, transport errors and chain mismatches. No separate indexer, runtime or environment was installed. Amounts represent the wallet's actual net balance change per coin type, including gas, with on-chain decimals and exact signed raw units in the description. Multiple recipients remain explicit rather than being guessed; a non-monetary object-change receipt has zero financial amount.

The real `SuiHistoryTests` passed 1/1 with zero skips after 53 signed native transfers, proving pagination beyond 50 entries, recipient-only incoming history, sender history, unique receipt identities, actual checkpoint dates, empty history for an unrelated wallet, wrong-chain failure and unavailable-node failure. The fixed report is `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-ledger-history.trx`. All 11 unit tests passed with zero skips in `sui-unit-tests.trx`; the old activation tests now prove an unreachable provider cannot activate instead of expecting unconfigured mainnet access to succeed. The unused HTTP client and obsolete uncompiled `contracts/oasis.move` prototype were removed. Old weak integration tests against an unconfigured mainnet provider were replaced by the existing real local storage/lifecycle tests, not retained as a passing-provider claim. Removed tracked sources remain recoverable from Git history.

The combined real storage/native/NFT/token/bridge/history suite passed 8/8 with zero skips in `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-provider-lifecycle.trx`. No environment or SDK duplicate was allocated; the retained local node was stopped after verification. This is a provider lifecycle checkpoint, not an all-endpoint or multi-provider V2 routing pass.

NFT minting now resolves a specified minting avatar through the existing wallet manager instead of silently using the configured system signer. All supplied recipient identifiers must resolve to the same wallet; conflicting address, avatar ID, username or email inputs fail. Burn requests use the shared signing-key resolver for private keys, seed phrases, public-key matching and avatar wallets, and validate a supplied OASIS NFT ID against the persisted metadata before submitting. These avatar-wallet paths still require an end-to-end fixture against the real wallet manager.

The expanded live NFT lifecycle test passed 1/1 with zero skips again on 2026-10-11 in `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-nft-request-options.trx`. It verifies the explicit `SuiObject` standard and persisted price, discount, royalty and basis points, sale dates and mint memo; rejects a mismatched NFT ID and public key; and burns a recipient-owned NFT using its real mnemonic. The mint memo is preserved in on-chain NFT metadata, not represented as a native Sui transaction memo. All 12 unit tests passed with zero skips in `sui-unit-tests.trx`, including rejection of invalid economic fields, inverted sale dates, a non-Sui provider and incompatible NFT standards before network access. An initial rerun omitted `OASIS_SUI_SDK_BRIDGE` and failed two wallet tests because the copied bridge could not resolve the installed SDK; correcting the environment to reuse the existing source SDK resolved both failures without reinstalling dependencies.

The full eight-test Sui .NET direct-provider suite was rerun on 2026-10-11 and passed 8/8, zero skips, in 3 minutes 21 seconds. `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-live.trx` covers native transfer/status, incoming/outgoing history, three storage/index/import/search/export paths, NFT request options and ownership lifecycle, native bridge transfers, and custom-token mint/transfer/escrow/unlock/explicit-amount burn. The V2 Sui routing filter then passed 2/2, zero skips, in 14 seconds, retained in `TestResults/HyperDriveSui/sui-v2-routing.trx`. It checks actual replication and measured provider selection against the Sui contract plus failover after closing the owned SDK transport, not a node outage. These runs reused chain ID `fd2b5bb4`, the existing 1.81.1 runtime, ledger and protected `/var/tmp/oasis-sui/deployment.json` fixture with its token-wallet sidecar. Fixture preparation verified reuse without publishing again. The installed source SDK was selected with `OASIS_SUI_SDK_BRIDGE`; no installation, download, new wallet fixture or ledger regeneration occurred.
