The exact owned node process was stopped afterward and its launch session exited successfully. Packaged SDK execution, full HTTP routes, all request options and wider failure/provider matrices remain gates.

The shared NFT standard enum now adds `SuiObject` after ERC721, ERC1155 and SPL, preserving the original numeric values. The Sui adapter accepts this standard and persists it in metadata. The generic NFT manager validates the standard/provider pair and rejects unknown enum values; its focused new regression tests are added but not yet executed. Independent nullable mint/send timeout overrides and wait flags now map to their corresponding request fields rather than checking unrelated options. The ONODE Core build passed before the latest standard-validation change; the updated manager still requires its build/test gate. Neither this source change nor the direct provider test proves the full HTTP mint route.

The generic mint path previously attempted a second transfer after Sui had already minted directly to the requested recipient. The adapter now compares the actual object's owner with the SDK-normalized recipient and reports the real combined mint/transfer digest through the existing `SendNFTTransactionResult` and `SendNFTTransactionHash` fields. The generic manager consumes that completed-transfer result and does not send the object again. The expanded test adds a direct mint to a different wallet and retains burn cleanup for that object. JavaScript syntax and diff checks passed; the changed C# path and these latest live assertions have not been executed while builds are held for disk cleanup.

Sui remains outside the complete-provider pass set. Avatar-addressed native transfers, complete NFT request-option coverage, broader storage failure/authorization checks, bridge coordinator integration and the full provider lifecycle matrix remain open. Custom-token and NFT history coverage also need explicit lifecycle assertions beyond the native-history test. The additive shared response and enum changes require the WEB4–WEB10 publish/compatibility gate before deployment or merge. These tests and changes do not close the all-provider failover/load-balancing/replication gate. Mysten's current client/signing contracts are documented in the [official gRPC client guide](https://sdk.mystenlabs.com/sui/clients/grpc) and [transaction execution guide](https://sdk.mystenlabs.com/sui/clients/executing).

### Shared token burn verification

The wallet manager rejects zero or negative burn amounts before provider lookup in both sync and async WEB4 calls. Core builds and four focused tests passed with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/wallet-token-burn.trx`, including field-preserving projection and rejection with no provider manager available. This prevents missing quantities reaching older provider defaults; live HTTP mapping and each provider's positive-amount semantics still require their own evidence.

Aptos now subtracts the requested positive burn amount from the configured signer's balance and stored supply, retaining the asset and other holders' balances instead of deleting the token record. Locked and insufficient-balance burns fail; supplied credentials must match the configured signer, and unresolved avatar IDs or seed phrases are rejected rather than ignored. The build passed and input-validation tests passed 3/3 with zero skips in `TestResults/AptosRuntime/aptos-explicit-burn-validation.trx`. The integration fixture now expects a 0.25 burn to retain 88.75 for the configured holder and 11 for the recipient, but this changed assertion has not run on-chain. This remains an SDK-signed update to custom Move-backed storage, not a native Aptos fungible-asset implementation. Client-side read/modify/write concurrency and avatar-wallet resolution remain gates. The older ten-test result does not certify the changed burn path; the provider is marked REVERIFY until current live evidence passes. Its evidence runner also requires fixed-environment reuse before another run.

The NEAR burn amount update requires live re-verification. Its previous contract deleted the complete token record, including balances held by other accounts. The updated contract subtracts only the explicit amount from the predecessor's own balance, preserves the asset and other holders, rejects locked/zero/insufficient burns and derives remaining supply from retained balances. The adapter sends exact eight-decimal units instead of rounding; the same conversion also applies to mint and transfer. The provider build, contract TypeScript type check and focused unit test passed; `TestResults/NearRuntime/near-exact-token-units.trx` contains 1/1 passing test, zero skips, covering precision rejection and exact conversion of decimal.MaxValue without intermediate decimal overflow. The integration fixture now asserts that a 0.25 burn leaves 89.75 with the caller and the other holder's 10 unchanged, but that updated assertion is not yet live evidence. The runner now locks and reuses `/var/tmp/oasis-near-evidence`, retains its compiler/SDK cache, reuses valid installed bridge dependencies and bounds .NET workers. The sandbox process and ledger lifecycle still need review before claiming full environment reuse; the old 12-test result does not certify this changed bytecode or explicit-amount behavior.

### TRON signed native and token transaction implementation

`TronRealProviderRoutingTests` passes 1/1 combined real-provider V2 test with zero skips in `TestResults/HyperDriveTron/tron-real-v2-routing.trx`. It verifies replication from LocalFile into encrypted TRON storage by direct readback and successful replication diagnostics, performance-based selection using measured reads from both providers, and successful failover to LocalFile during an actual pause of the owned TRON node. During that outage, a LocalFile save succeeds with a warning and one failed replica; after resume, direct TRON readback confirms the replica is absent. A new explicit save repairs it. Healthy hard deletion removes both copies; deletion during another pause succeeds at LocalFile with a warning while the TRON copy remains after resume. Cleanup deletes the three owned records from both stores where present and deactivates the providers. The runtime is paused afterward. This proves partial-save and partial-delete diagnostics and divergence for the LocalFile/TRON pairing, not automatic replay or reconciliation. All-provider combinations, all operation types, authenticated HTTP routes and durable recovery remain gates.

One reusable local TRON runtime is installed in the existing WSL root Podman environment: `oasis-tron-tre`, pinned to `docker.io/tronbox/tre@sha256:cbf2973095933e3ae3237767082e29add2f9b647d7a4aebdfb7a953c6e5656ba` (TRE 2.0.0, java-tron 4.8.2). The cached image is 278,119,115 bytes. HTTP 9090 and gRPC 50051/50052 bind to loopback only; memory is capped at 2 GiB, CPUs at two, and logs at 10 MiB. Public local fixture keys are used only against the pinned private-chain genesis. No mainnet or public-testnet assets were spent.

`TronLocalSdkTests` passes 6/6 with zero skips in `TestResults/TronRuntime/tron-local-sdk.trx`: native transfer, deployed TVM contract CRUD/pagination, encrypted storage reopening, provider holon lifecycle/deletion, and a non-enumerable NFT fixture's mint/metadata/ownership discovery/transfer/burn. NFT discovery validates actual block/transaction/log order, including history supplied out of order, and ownership/balance checks after transfer and burn. The same NFT contract and exact large token ID are reused; interrupted owned fixtures are reconciled before remint, and burn removes the test NFT afterward. Tests use only public local fixture accounts; the recipient requires a local TRX energy budget. This is real SDK/TVM execution, not proof of complete arbitrary collection standards, provider avatar-wallet resolution, HTTP authorization or source-receipt bridge verification. Authenticated routes, node restart recovery and broader V2 operation coverage remain gates.

The additional NFT escrow test executes the shipping `OASISTokenEscrow` bytecode on TVM through signed SDK calls. It verifies approval and custody, an unapproved deposit rejected without changing ownership, another account's withdrawal rejected, withdrawal by the original depositor, and a second withdrawal rejected. One fixed approval-capable NFT fixture is reused separately because the older deployed fixture lacks approval and transferFrom. The test reconciles its fixed token after interrupted runs, preserves unrelated ownership, and unlocks/burns the owned fixture afterward. This proves SDK contract execution, not authenticated avatar wallet resolution or HTTP authorization.

DNA exposes `ContractFeeLimitSun`, propagated through bootloader registration to the owned SDK runtime. Its default remains 100,000,000 SUN; nonpositive values fail before network allocation. Local holon persistence explicitly uses 1,000,000,000 SUN because the encrypted payload exceeded the original ceiling. Production/Railway configuration is unchanged. This is an authorized maximum, not the fee necessarily charged. Constant calls may omit contractRet; explicit failures remain rejected and committed receipts still require SUCCESS.

Use `Scripts/TestHosts/Use-ReusableTronRuntime.ps1 -Action Resume` before tests and `-Action Pause` afterward. The script reuses the same container, accounts, image and ledger, never recreates a stopped instance automatically, and requires explicit `-InstallMissing` if the pin is absent. TRE startup is not safely repeatable on an existing container, so pause/unpause preserves the initialized instance and stops idle block/log growth. An exited instance requires investigation, not an automatic fresh setup. The runtime is paused after this verification run. No separate download archive was retained.

TRON avatar, avatar-detail, holon, version, search, import and export storage now inherits `KeyValueStorageProviderBase` over `TronPrivateStorageBackend` and the signed SDK `TronStorageBackend`. The superseded unsigned/truncated storage overrides were removed. The shared `OASISKeyValueStore` contract supplies exact ABI reads, signed puts/deletes and revision-checked 256-key pagination. Keys and values use the shared HMAC index and AES-GCM composition, not plaintext public records. Activation requires a verified SDK network and encrypted owned probe with committed readback and deletion. Storage operations share the runtime lifecycle lock; owned encryption/index keys are cleared on failed activation or deactivation and reconstructed on reactivation using the same persistent external key. Injected backends remain caller-owned.

DNA and bootloader registration expose `StorageContractAddress`, `StorageOwnerAddress`, `StorageSigningKeyEnvironmentVariable` and `StorageEncryptionKeyEnvironmentVariable`, separate from the fungible-token contract. The variable names default to `OASIS_TRON_STORAGE_PRIVATE_KEY` and `OASIS_TRON_STORAGE_ENCRYPTION_KEY`; the latter must contain base64 for an externally persisted 32-byte key. Secrets are resolved lazily, never stored in DNA. Missing or partial configuration fails explicitly. The configured backend uses the provider's existing SDK client, not a second runtime. Back up the encryption key; rotation and migration are not implemented.

NFT minting now calls the explicitly selected `mint(address,uint256,string)` collection extension through local SDK signing and a solidified successful receipt. `MetaData` must contain `MintFunctionSignature` with that exact signature, `TokenAddress` and an unsigned decimal `TokenId`; `JSONMetaDataURL` supplies the URI and `MintedByAvatarId` resolves the minter wallet. One token is minted per call, directly to `SendToAddressAfterMinting` or the minter. No contract, ID or URI is fabricated. Batch minting, collection deployment, royalties, metadata freeze and avatar-recipient resolution remain open; unsupported requested options fail explicitly.

The current offline TRON suite passes 174/174 tests with zero skips in `TestResults/TronRuntime/tron-unit.trx`. It covers storage ABI encoding/decoding, exact Unicode and large counters, malformed payloads, cancellation before credentials, key-buffer clearing, missing configuration, SDK transaction safety, native NFT escrow encoding, receipt-log NFT discovery and indexed-history pagination. DNA, TRON and bootloader bounded builds pass. Offline tests do not prove storage deployment, encrypted persistence/restart, authorization, NFT mint consensus or real V2 failover/load balancing/replication. The new configuration has not been deployed or merged into Development.

Wallet history now uses confirmed paginated native transaction hashes followed by SDK transaction-identity and solidified-receipt verification. The API preserves native hashes in `NativeTransactionId`, exact integer values in `RawAmount`, the unit in `AmountUnit` and the protobuf operation name in `NativeOperation`; it does not fabricate an OASIS GUID. The mapping and interface JSON test preserves 9,007,199,254,740,993 SUN exactly. The live native transfer/history test passes 1/1 with zero skips in `TestResults/TronRuntime/tron-local-history.trx`. Mapping currently supports native transfers, smart-contract calls and contract creation only. Confirmed failures retain `NativeExecutionResult` and `NativeError`, including the actual rejected escrow transaction's native hash. Amounts represent the requested value, not proof of an applied balance change; failed deployments may have no created contract address. Missing/default VM results are not inferred successful. Other native operation types and end-to-end indexed failed-history coverage remain completion gates.

`TronGridShastaHistoryTests` passes 1/1 real public Shasta read-only integration test with zero skips in `TestResults/TronRuntime/tron-shasta-history.trx`. It checks the configured genesis, follows actual fingerprints using two-record pages and preserves a fixed upper timestamp. The indexer returned 18 historical records, including one with empty `token_info`; the reader preserves such records rather than silently discarding them. Advertised pagination without a fingerprint, repeated cursors, unsuccessful pages and out-of-bound timestamps fail explicitly. This verifies indexed history retrieval only, not complete NFT inventory or signed transactions. Discovery must separately establish token classification and current ownership through the SDK.

NFT minting uses the explicit collection extension `mint(address,uint256,string)`. Native custody withdrawal/release, `ownerOf` and `tokenURI` reads, and explicit collection-owner enumeration now use the SDK instead of fabricated metadata or unsigned writes. Enumeration requires the collection's enumerable extension and checks ownership and the final balance; it is not a block-pinned snapshot. Wallet-wide NFT inventory, source receipt validation/replay protection and live TVM execution remain gates. A public history response cannot satisfy these gates.

The wallet-wide NFT methods discover native token identities through confirmed paginated history and SDK-validated solidified receipts. Four-topic Transfer logs retain exact uint256 IDs, parties and log positions. Receipt blocks establish verified transaction membership and order; replay uses block/transaction/log order, excluding final transfers away or burns. Current ownerOf checks and collection balance checks before/after reading validate remaining candidates without the enumerable extension. Changed ownership or balance mismatch fails instead of returning partial inventory. Entirely unindexed collections and same-count concurrent changes remain possible; complete block-pinned coverage, arbitrary collection compatibility and authenticated API tests remain gates. The non-enumerable local TVM fixture above supplies real receipt integration evidence.

`LockNFTAsync` and `UnlockNFTAsync` now resolve the avatar's TRON signing wallet and call `lockNFT(address,uint256)` or `unlockNFT(address,uint256)` on the separately configured `EscrowContractAddress`. `NFTTokenAddress` must be the native `contract/unsigned-decimal-token-id`; `Web3NFTId` is never substituted as a native token ID. The updated `OASISTokenEscrow` contract records the original depositor and permits only that depositor to withdraw, verifies ownership before/after transfer, and rolls back its record on failed transfer. NFT approval must already authorize the escrow; these calls do not grant approval implicitly. Existing deployments must be upgraded/replaced with this contract version before these methods can execute. The shared local EVM runner passes 40 assertions across fungible-token and NFT escrow, including unauthorized withdrawal, failure rollback, repeat withdrawal, zero and maximum uint256 IDs. This is real EVM contract execution, not TRON TVM or authenticated API verification. Direct NFT transfers to escrow bypassing `lockNFT` do not create a withdrawable deposit.

TRON has direct SDK/TVM and LocalFile-paired V2 evidence described above, but the complete operation and authenticated-route matrix remains incomplete and is not release-ready. The bounded provider build passes with zero errors. The current focused offline SDK suite passes **96/96, zero skips**, in `TestResults/TronRuntime/tron-sdk-safety.trx`. These tests validate transaction encoding, receipt checks, configuration and concurrency; they do not prove signed execution on TRON, authenticated HTTP routes or V2 routing.

Transaction status now uses genesis-verified SDK full-node and solidity-node RPCs rather than the unsigned HTTP parser. Pending requires a full-node transaction whose SHA256 raw-data identity matches the requested ID; an empty response is NotFound. Completed requires matching solidified transaction identity, a committed block receipt and successful execution. TriggerSmartContract and CreateSmartContract also require VM SUCCESS. Invalid hashes and pre-cancelled requests fail before activation. Four new offline tests cover absent transaction data, exact identity, tamper rejection and invalid request hashes; the existing receipt tests cover failed/default/missing results. A real rejected TVM escrow receipt returns the appended `BridgeTransactionStatus.Failed` value; unknown or default VM outcomes remain errors. Regression coverage distinguishes completed, failed and ambiguous receipts. Live pending transitions, deployed HTTP behavior and expired status mapping remain gates. These statuses describe the native-chain leg only, not cross-chain settlement.

Native bridge WithdrawAsync and DepositAsync now use the shared signed SDK transfer and solidified receipt confirmation. Withdrawal signs with the explicit sender key and sends to the configured pool wallet; deposit resolves the configured pool signer and sends to the receiver. A missing pool, missing identity/key or nonpositive/fractional SUN amount fails explicitly. The former unsigned JSON signing helper and invented pool address are removed. Completed describes only the confirmed native-chain leg, not cross-chain settlement. BridgePoolWalletAddress now supplies only the native pool wallet; ContractAddress supplies the token contract and EscrowContractAddress supplies token escrow. DNA exposes all three and the bootloader forwards each independently. Provider, DNA and bootloader builds pass. Authenticated pool-release authorization, real wallet balances, live transactions and settlement orchestration remain gates. Five offline tests cover amount/configuration rejection before activation, including refusal to reuse a configured token contract as a native pool; no funded transfer was performed.

TRON account creation and mnemonic restoration now use the cached NBitcoin 9.0.3 BIP39/BIP32 implementation with the standard m/44'/195'/0'/0/0 path, then derive the uncompressed secp256k1 public key through Nethereum. Generated mnemonics restore the identical signing key. Invalid words/checksums and raw hex passed as a mnemonic are rejected rather than hashed into unrelated credentials. These are local key operations and do not activate provider channels or claim that an on-chain account exists. Pre-cancelled requests produce no credentials. Six focused tests cover recovery, rejection, cancellation and a public known-answer vector independently derived with cached ethers. The reusable runner `Scripts/TestHosts/verify-tron-mnemonic-vector.cjs` and the C# regression test agree on both private and uncompressed public keys. No real wallet credentials are used. Public API authorization, address-return API design and on-chain account activation remain gates. Placeholder SHA256 key/address derivation and the unrelated random mnemonic generator are removed.

Native transfers by address, avatar ID, username and email use the reused TronNet 0.2.0 SDK, node-built protobuf transactions, local signing, one broadcast and solidified receipt confirmation. Email delegates to the same address transaction path after wallet resolution. Exact int64 SUN conversion rejects overflow and fractional units. Both GetBalanceAsync and GetAccountBalanceAsync use the shared solidity-node GetAccount RPC after genesis verification, require matching account identity and reject missing or negative balances rather than reporting successful zero. The account-specific method propagates cancellation to SDK reads and rejects pre-cancelled requests before activation. SUN conversion is exact decimal internally; GetAccountBalanceAsync preserves decimal output, while the shared balance interface requires double output. These requests expose native TRX only.

TRC20 transfer, mint and burn share signed SDK execution. They query actual contract decimals and convert explicit amounts to exact uint256 units. Transfer encodes transfer(address,uint256); mint encodes mint(address,uint256); burn encodes burn(uint256). Node-built owner, contract, call data and timestamps are validated before signing. Transfer requires exactly one 32-byte return equal to true; void mint returns are allowed, but a returned boolean must be true. A matching solidified receipt must have successful native execution and, for contracts, VM SUCCESS rather than DEFAULT. The fee ceiling is 100,000,000 SUN, separate from the amount. Errors after broadcast retain the original transaction ID and rejection reason; no replacement is silently submitted.

Mint resolves the requested avatar's detached default signing wallet and targets an existing configured mint-capable contract. It does not deploy a token or implement symbol, title, description, tags or metadata changes. Username and email TRC20 transfers resolve avatar identities and detached default wallets, then delegate to the signed transfer route using the explicit token argument as contract address. Explicit-key routes validate signer ownership and optional public-key correspondence and reject seed phrases. Actual wallet authorization, mint permissions, balance/supply changes and HTTP integration remain verification gates.

NFT transfer now encodes safeTransferFrom(address,address,uint256), preserves exact uint256 identifiers including zero, verifies the signer owns the sender and queries ownerOf before execution. A missing transfer recipient is rejected and cannot select the shared burn path. NFT burn requires the canonical native NFT address `contract/unsigned-decimal-token-id`, queries ownerOf, and signs the contract's burn(uint256) extension. An OASIS Web3NFTId GUID is never substituted for a native token ID. Both operations return only a transaction ID confirmed by the shared solidified receipt path. Burn is available only on contracts implementing that extension. Native identity production during mint/load, Web3NFTId lookup, approved-operator support, live receiver behavior, post-operation ownership checks and request-specific wait/timeout semantics remain open; these operations are not full NFT-interface completion.

`TronSdkRuntime` owns one service container and reusable full-node and solidity-node channels. Public endpoints require TLS; plaintext is restricted to loopback. Both nodes must return the configured genesis block ID. Activation, signed operations, reads and deactivation share a lifecycle lock. `ExecuteRuntimeAsync<T>` rechecks active state after acquiring the lock and holds it through the operation; deactivation cannot dispose channels mid-operation. Failed activation closes owned channels. Offline tests cover queued deactivation, successful/failed operation completion, lock release and inactive-runtime refusal.

Locking reads the signer's spendable token balance and allowance, confirms an exact approval when necessary, then signs lockAll(token) once. A different nonzero allowance requires explicit revocation rather than silent replacement. A deposit failure after confirmed approval retains the approval ID and original deposit error; remaining allowance requires reconciliation, not silent retry. Unlocking resolves the requesting avatar's detached default signing wallet, reads locked(token, owner), rejects an empty deposit, signs unlock(token) and requires the confirmed returned amount to match the deposit.

`Contracts/OASISTokenEscrow.sol` accounts separately for each token and depositor. No administrator can withdraw user deposits. Reentrancy is guarded, failed token calls revert, and exact sender/escrow balance changes reject fee-on-transfer deposits and withdrawals. Full-balance locking reflects the shared request's absence of an amount field; partial locking requires an API extension. Approval supports exact raw uint256 allowance and explicit zero revocation.

`node Scripts/TestHosts/verify-tron-escrow-evm.cjs` uses the existing Solidity 0.8.26 compiler and in-process Hardhat EVM with Paris bytecode. Twenty-six assertions pass, covering allowance requirements, owner-only withdrawals, deposit/refund balances, failed transfer and taxed transfer rollback, two independent depositors and repeated unlock rejection. The runner selects the local network, downloads nothing and retains no disk-backed ledger. This is EVM contract evidence, not TRON TVM deployment or signed SDK execution. Live approval/deposit failure sequencing, TVM state changes, authenticated avatar authorization, Web3TokenId mapping, HTTP routes, cross-chain settlement and real TRON V2 failover/load balancing/replication remain open.

OASISDNA and bootloader expose ContractAddress, BridgePoolWalletAddress, EscrowContractAddress, FullNodeGrpcEndpoint, SolidityNodeGrpcEndpoint, GenesisBlockId, SigningKeyEnvironmentVariable and ApiKeyEnvironmentVariable. Credential settings name environment variables, never signing keys. The configured signer must own the requested sender; a single configured signer does not prove avatar-specific signing support. The same pinned local TRON runtime supplies the direct SDK and V2 evidence above; no public-chain assets have been spent. Account activation, bridge settlement, remaining NFT options and authenticated operation coverage remain gates.

### Sui live lifecycle verification

Confirmed failure reporting passes 1/1 live test with zero skips in `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-failed-status-live.trx`. `SdkBridge/test-failed-status.mjs` uses the retained synthetic issuer/package and official SDK to resolve the transaction kind, select a real gas coin, and sign resolved bytes for an intentional zero-amount mint abort. The transaction commits with failed effects; the provider returns `Failed`, not a lookup error. `OASIS_SUI_TEST_FAILED_DIGEST` supplies that original digest to the C# readback test, allowing repeated verification without resubmission. The separate native transfer/completed-status test passes 1/1 in `sui-status-live.trx`. The root-owned retained ledger was restarted under its existing owner and stopped after testing; no genesis, fixture publication, runtime or SDK installation was repeated.

Sui's signed execution errors now retain the original failed digest and require reconciliation before retry. Bridge regressions pass 6/6 with zero skips. Status mapping regressions pass Sui 5/5 and Cosmos 4/4, including missing/malformed boolean and invalid native-code rejection. Cosmos maps confirmed nonzero execution codes to `Failed` rather than `Canceled`; its live readback passes 1/1 with zero skips in `TestResults/CosmosRuntime/cosmos-failed-status-live.trx`. The official SDK submits an intentionally overdrawn local bank transfer with an explicit gas/fee ceiling, establishes a committed nonzero execution code, and the provider independently reads that original hash as `Failed`. The retained hash permits repeat readback without resubmission. The same wasmd ledger and runtime are preserved. Authenticated HTTP behavior and complete cross-provider operation coverage remain release gates.

The existing Sui 1.81.1 local ledger was reused on 2026-10-11 without regeneration or a new runtime installation. The Sui provider compiled with zero errors. Native transfer and history integration tests passed **2/2, zero skips**, retained in `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-native-history.trx`. This run proves signed native transfer confirmation, exact balance changes, signer and chain mismatch rejection, incoming/outgoing history across 53 transfers, and failure propagation for an unavailable RPC.

NFT, token, storage and bridge integration tests passed **6/6, zero skips**, in `TestResults/SuiRuntime/sui-lifecycle.trx`. They cover batch NFT minting, metadata, direct mint to another recipient, transfer and burn cleanup; token mint, transfer, escrow, unlock, burn and authorization; avatar/detail and holon persistence with indexes and versions; import/search/export/proximity; and actual bridge custody with receipt binding and stale-receipt rejection. Two NFT assertions now access the existing concrete response field through the returned response type. These direct-provider tests do not prove the complete HTTP or generic manager pipelines.

`SdkBridge/prepare-test-fixture.mjs` retains one synthetic fixture outside disposable report directories. The current fixture is `/var/tmp/oasis-sui/deployment.json` with its token wallet sidecar, both restricted to mode 0600. A new local publication exposed an SDK response parsing defect: `objectTypes` includes the literal `package`, which is not a Move struct tag. The parser now handles that defined type correctly. The committed publication was reconciled through the publisher's actual capability and receipt, then its pending currency registration was finalized without publishing again. Explicit reconciliation never submits another publication; uncertain fixtures are otherwise rejected. Re-running fixture preparation verified reuse. JavaScript bridge tests passed **6/6, zero skips**, including this parser regression.

### Hedera SDK implementation and verification

The existing official SDK bridge now supports FileCreateTransaction, FileUpdateTransaction, FileDeleteTransaction and FileContentsQuery. File creation assigns the signing public key as its access key. Mutations use an explicit payer transaction ID, explicit positive int64 tinybar fee ceiling, local signing and a SUCCESS consensus receipt; submission is limited to one attempt and transaction-ID regeneration is disabled. Errors retain the original mutation ID for reconciliation. Reads require an explicit query-payment ceiling and strict UTF-8 decoding. Supplied public-key mismatches, payer mismatches and missing fee limits fail before opening the client. File mutation contents are currently limited to 2,048 UTF-8 bytes and are never silently truncated; larger records require a complete chunking design.

The bridge also implements storage reads through `ContractCallQuery` and signed puts/deletes through `ContractExecuteTransaction`, using the ABI in `Providers/Shared/Contracts/OASISKeyValueStore.sol`. Queries require explicit gas and payment limits. Writes require an explicit payer transaction ID, gas and fee ceiling, validate the signing public key when supplied, disable ID regeneration and limit submission to one attempt. Only a SUCCESS consensus receipt establishes completion; errors retain the original transaction ID for reconciliation. Enumeration uses bounded 256-key pages at one contract revision, checks cursor/total consistency and duplicates, and fails if the revision changes rather than returning an incomplete scan.

The bridge suites pass 39/39 offline tests with zero skips, including file and contract transaction serialization, signatures, exact Unicode payloads, large integer pagination, payer/fee checks and pre-network credential rejection. Signed transfers and HTS mutations also retain one submission attempt and disable transaction-ID regeneration. `node Scripts/TestHosts/verify-shared-keyvalue-evm.cjs` passes ownership, CRUD, empty-versus-missing values, deletion/reinsertion and revision-checked pagination assertions on the existing in-process Hardhat EVM. This is contract behavior evidence, not Hedera consensus or TRON TVM proof. The runner uses cached dependencies and retains no disk ledger.

HashgraphOASIS now inherits `KeyValueStorageProviderBase` over `HederaStorageBackend` composed with mandatory `PrivateKeyValueBackend`. Logical keys become HMAC-SHA256 indexes using a domain-separated key derived from the persistent encryption key. Both logical keys and values are stored inside authenticated encrypted records; enumeration decrypts those records, validates physical/logical identity and returns ordinally sorted matching keys. Record counts, sizes and access patterns remain public. The superseded mirror-node avatar/detail/holon CRUD, search/import/export implementations and synthetic activation overrides were removed. Activation authenticates an existing record when present, then requires an encrypted put, committed readback, delete and absence check. A wrong persistent key fails before new probe writes. Missing configuration produces an OASIS error instead of success. Offline key generation still works without a storage deployment.

The encryption layer uses AES-256-GCM with a random 12-byte nonce, 16-byte authentication tag and storage key bound as authenticated data. It preserves full entity payloads, rejects plaintext or unauthenticated records, copies the supplied 32-byte key and clears retained encryption/index keys on disposal. The shared storage suite passes 25/25 tests with zero skips, including private-index avatar passwords/details and holon versions, restart recovery, ordered prefix enumeration, tampering, wrong-key activation without mutations, moved ciphertext, empty/missing values, owned probe cleanup and disposal. The Hedera offline filter passes 53/53 tests with zero skips, including missing-config activation/CRUD rejection, cancellation before secret resolution and existing key/NFT/token/mirror-read regressions. The offline filter excludes every `HederaTestnet` class. Reports are `TestResults/SharedKeyValue/shared-storage.trx` and `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/hedera-offline.trx`. DNA, provider and bootloader bounded builds pass.

OASISDNA `HashgraphOASIS` exposes `StorageContractId`, `StorageOperatorAccountId`, `StorageSigningKeyType`, `StorageGasLimit`, `StorageMaxFeeTinybars`, `StorageSigningKeyEnvironmentVariable` and `StorageEncryptionKeyEnvironmentVariable`. The last two default to `OASIS_HEDERA_STORAGE_PRIVATE_KEY` and `OASIS_HEDERA_STORAGE_ENCRYPTION_KEY`. The encryption environment variable must contain base64 for a persistent, externally managed 32-byte key; signing uses the explicit configured algorithm. JSON stores variable names, not secrets. Back up the encryption key securely: generating a replacement key does not recover existing ciphertext, and key rotation/migration is not implemented. Bootloader registration forwards the settings and snapshots variable names.

The funded-testnet storage test is implemented and compiles, but has not run. It requires `OASIS_HEDERA_TEST_ACCOUNT_ID`, `OASIS_HEDERA_TEST_PRIVATE_KEY` (DER), `OASIS_HEDERA_TEST_STORAGE_CONTRACT_ID`, `OASIS_HEDERA_TEST_STORAGE_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (base64), `OASIS_HEDERA_TEST_STORAGE_GAS_LIMIT` and `OASIS_HEDERA_TEST_STORAGE_MAX_FEE_TINYBARS`. It exercises activation, avatar/detail/holon persistence, provider restart with the same key, and confirmed-owned record deletion followed by missing-key reads. Missing settings fail explicitly rather than skip or produce mock evidence.

The shared contract is deployed and exercised on the reusable TRON TVM runtime; Hedera deployment and consensus verification remain outstanding. Concurrent multi-key updates, enumeration/read snapshot consistency, large-record gas limits, authorization, encrypted live readback, live restart/key recovery, lifecycle concurrency and real V2 orchestration remain open. Geospatial placeholder queries and unused legacy client helpers still require removal or implementation; file lifetime/renewal remains open for any file-service storage. Funded-testnet account, private-key and recipient environment variables remain absent in process, user and machine scopes; no paid query or mutation has been executed. These changes have not been deployed or merged into Development.

The ONODE multi-mint path no longer automatically calls `MintNFTAsync` again after a failed or uncertain result. It submits once per requested iteration and returns an explicit reconciliation error, including the provider's original transaction ID when available, instead of putting error text in the mint-hash field. ONODE builds successfully with bounded workers and `BuildProjectReferences=false`, reusing the existing STAR DNA source at `C:\Source\OASIS\STAR ODK` on the pinned STAR ODK commit. The earlier 120 missing STAR/STARDNA errors came from the task checkout's absent STAR source and missing cached package. The existing STAR DNA project built without restoring; a non-recursive ONODE restore replenished missing dependencies in the shared cache. `StarDnaProjectPath` selects an existing source project, and an invalid explicit path fails clearly. No STAR clone or second runtime was created.

The ONODE unit-test project now explicitly declares `IsTestProject=true`. The NFT standard validation filter executed 15 tests, all passing with zero skips, and produced `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/onode-nft-standard.trx`. Its Ethereum cases register an offline EVM-capable mock in an isolated test collection and unregister only that owned mock; they do not activate a network provider or spend gas. The earlier command that exited without a report did not execute these tests. These results prove standard validation only. Signer forwarding, live consensus and full HTTP orchestration remain open.

Each manager-owned batch iteration now calls a single-mint helper that sets the provider request's `NumberToMint` and `Amount` to one and restores both original values in `finally`, including on provider exceptions. Four focused tests cover unspecified quantity, two larger quantities and exception restoration; they verify exactly one provider call per helper invocation. The combined quantity, delivery and standard filters passed 23 tests with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/onode-nft-quantity.trx`, and the bounded ONODE build passed. This prevents a provider from minting the full batch on each manager iteration, but does not prove concurrent reuse of the same request instance, live consensus or full HTTP orchestration.

The manager now records and attempts metadata persistence for each confirmed NFT inside the batch loop, saving the parent on the final iteration rather than dropping all earlier assets. Both response lists retain recorded assets if a later provider call fails; an asset included in a provider error is also retained for reconciliation without incrementing the confirmed count. NFT metadata projection copies request collections and preserves provider metadata keys instead of replacing native identifiers or sharing transient batch markers with the request. Four focused tests cover one and five recorded assets, a retained partial asset and metadata isolation. All four NFT test filters passed 27 tests with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/onode-nft-batch.trx`; the bounded ONODE build passed. Partial asset entries are not proof of successful consensus or delivery.

Full `MintNFTInternalAsync` tests execute the manager's batch loop and the real V2 `HolonManager` route using an isolated provider registry and mock storage/NFT providers. Nine cases cover three successful mints, failed metadata storage, failure on the second mint with and without an asset, and five delivery branches: success, provider error, null result, exception and an already-confirmed delivery that must not be resubmitted. They assert exact mint/save/send call counts, native NFT address projection, retained asset identities, both response lists, confirmed counts, restored request quantities and original transaction IDs. The failed-storage case exposed a false success: metadata errors were warnings and the final formatter replaced their message. Metadata failures now set `IsError=true` and leave `IsSaved=false`, preserve minted assets and retain reconciliation messages; successful parent persistence sets `IsSaved=true` only if no earlier error occurred. Each uncertain delivery ID is retained on its asset under `NFT.UnconfirmedDeliveryTransactionId`, without setting a confirmed send hash. All five NFT filters passed 36 tests with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/onode-nft-orchestration.trx`; the bounded ONODE build passed. This proves the tested manager branches and isolated V2 mock routing, not signing-wallet resolution, a live chain, durable provider storage, HTTP endpoints or real provider failover/load balancing/replication.

Post-mint delivery now submits exactly once, without the former sleep/resubmission loop. Its submission helper preserves provider responses, including an original delivery transaction ID on error, and converts null responses or thrown exceptions into explicit reconciliation errors. The caller reports failed delivery as an error and no longer stores error prose as a transaction hash. Native NFT identity uses `FromNFTTokenAddress`, rather than putting a composite token/serial into the collection address field. Four focused mock tests prove one call for success, error, null and exception; together with standard validation, 19 tests passed with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/onode-nft-submission.trx`. The bounded ONODE build passed. These tests do not prove live delivery, signer lookup, batch persistence or the full HTTP route.

Confirmed Hedera mint-and-delivery responses now set both `SendNFTTransactionResult` and the NFT's `SendNFTTransactionHash` from the same successful transfer receipt. The shared response interface now exposes the send field already present on its concrete class. This prevents the ONODE projection from overwriting a completed delivery marker with an empty value and treating it as unsent. Core/provider builds and two focused response-contract tests passed with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/hedera-nft-delivery-response.trx`; the tests reproduce the consumer copy pattern, not the full ONODE route or a live delivery. Automatic mint and post-mint delivery resubmission loops have been removed, but the generic send constructor still omits signer fields. Full orchestration remains an open gate.

The standalone C# NFT-send entry points now share the signed HTS `nftTransfer` bridge operation. They consume the shared signer fields, require exactly one identified serial, reject conflicting asset identifiers and seed phrases, and return a transaction only after a SUCCESS receipt. The provider build and five boundary tests passed with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/hedera-nft-send-validation.trx`. This supersedes the older standalone-method gap descriptions below: the shared API contract and provider consumption are implemented, but ONODE forwarding, authenticated wallet lookup, HTTP binding, ownership/signature checks on a live ledger and independent transfer confirmation remain open. No live NFT transfer has been proven by these validation tests.

The shared WEB3/WEB4 NFT-send request now exposes private-key, public-key and seed-phrase fields. The wallet-manager projection preserves these alongside asset identifiers, quantity and memo. For same-chain Hedera sends without an explicit key, the manager resolves the authenticated avatar's detached default signing wallet and requires its account to match the sender; it does not infer credentials from an account address. Core builds passed for both target frameworks and two projection tests passed with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/wallet-nft-send.trx`. These tests do not exercise authenticated wallet lookup. Standalone Hedera method consumption, ONODE forwarding, HTTP binding, authorization and live signing remain gates.

NFT reads now use the provider's configured mirror endpoint and the native `tokens/{token}/nfts/{serial}` route, with the same `token/serial` identifier used by burn and mint results. Both entry points share this reader. It validates returned identity, deletion and owner fields, retains exact base64 metadata and exposes text/URLs only when bytes round-trip as UTF-8. It no longer creates a hard-coded mainnet client or treats collection data as an individual NFT. The provider build and six explicit HTTP-fixture contract tests passed with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/hedera-nft-read-contract.trx`. These tests prove request routing and response handling, not a live ledger read. Standalone NFT transfer still needs a shared request/API upgrade to carry an authorized signer; wallet addresses alone are not signing credentials.

The SDK bridge now supports native HTS NFT mint, serial-number burn and NFT transfer transactions. It validates unique positive int64 serial strings and explicit metadata, queries the actual asset type, enforces the mint/burn treasury destination, signs once and requires a SUCCESS consensus receipt. Mint responses expose the receipt's serials. Its 22 JavaScript tests pass with zero skips, including official SDK serialization/deserialization of metadata, burn serials above JavaScript's safe integer limit and NFT transfer bytes containing no HBAR or fungible-token amounts. These are offline SDK tests, not consensus evidence. The standalone C# transfer methods still call the old generic transaction client; replacing them and verifying the public NFT routes on funded testnet remain implementation gates. No additional runtime or dependencies were installed for this change.

Both C# mint entry points now share a signed HTS mint path using an existing NFT collection identified by `CollectionPublicKey`. It accepts one to ten serials, validates exact UTF-8 metadata and quantity, resolves every supplied recipient selector before minting and requires the selectors to agree. Receipt serials form native `token/serial` addresses; the first address and complete `HederaSerials` list are returned with the mint transaction ID. A requested recipient transfer uses the SDK's NFT transfer operation; if that later operation fails, the completed mint remains in the error result for reconciliation rather than being erased or automatically reminted. The bounded provider build and nine validation tests passed with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/hedera-nft-mint-validation.trx`. Actual mint/transfer consensus and this partial-failure behavior require live verification. Collection creation, larger batches, metadata-authority and royalty operations, native HTS enum exposure, and ONODE retry/result handling remain gates. Unsupported collection changes or another chain's NFT standard produce explicit errors rather than a false success.

Both C# NFT-burn entry points now use the signed SDK operation instead of posting to an invented mirror-node mutation endpoint. `NFTTokenAddress` identifies the native asset as `token/serial`, for example `0.0.1234/1`; `Web3NFTId` remains the OASIS GUID carried in the response, not an HTS serial. The burn resolves the avatar's detached default treasury wallet, rejects conflicting explicit private keys and seed phrases, validates public-key agreement through the SDK and requires a successful receipt. The provider build passed and ten validation tests passed with zero skips in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/hedera-nft-burn-validation.trx`. These tests verify input boundaries before wallet lookup; actual avatar-wallet resolution, supply-key authorization, treasury ownership and live NFT destruction remain funded-testnet gates.

HashgraphOASIS now uses one pinned official Hiero JavaScript SDK installation, `@hiero-ledger/sdk` 2.86.2, in its existing provider directory. Key generation and restoration return actual SDK private/public DER keys without inventing an account ID. Native HBAR and fungible HTS transfers use signed SDK transactions and require a successful consensus receipt. HBAR amounts use exact integer tinybars; HTS precision comes from a real token-info query. Signing credentials travel through standard input rather than process arguments. Failed or uncertain transfers retain the original transaction ID for reconciliation before retrying.

The mirror balance reader normalizes the endpoint to one `/api/v1/` prefix and reads the actual `balance.balance` tinybar field. Public mainnet mirror read tests passed **2/2, zero skips**, in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/hedera-mirror-balance.trx`. Official SDK JavaScript safety tests passed **10/10, zero skips**. The .NET SDK key and provider validation tests passed **12/12, zero skips**, in `TestResults/HashgraphRuntime/hedera-sdk-keys.trx`; they cover key round-trips, invalid HBAR precision, missing signing keys, secret-safe SDK failures retaining the transaction ID, local consensus configuration guards, invalid receipt IDs producing errors rather than false authoritative NotFound results, and mint asset/amount/avatar validation before wallet lookup.

HTS minting now uses `TokenMintTransaction` and the requested `Amount`, rather than a nonexistent mirror-write endpoint and a hard-coded one-token quantity. `MetaData.TokenAddress` identifies the existing fungible HTS asset. The minting avatar's default wallet supplies credentials through the detached decryption path. Amount precision comes from `TokenInfoQuery`; the requested recipient (optional `MetaData.MintToWalletAddress`, otherwise the avatar wallet) must match the actual treasury because HTS minting credits that treasury. Consensus must report success. This path builds but has no live mint evidence yet, including its avatar-wallet signing and supply-key authorization.

HTS burning now uses signed `TokenBurnTransaction`, ledger decimal precision and the burning avatar's detached treasury-wallet credentials. The shared WEB3/WEB4 burn request exposes `Amount`; zero and negative amounts are rejected rather than becoming a default one-token burn. Both wallet-manager mappings retain amount, avatar, asset identity and credentials, submit once and preserve the provider result without automatic rebroadcast after an uncertain transaction. Core and Hedera builds passed; mapping tests passed 2/2, amount-validation tests 2/2 and SDK safety tests 11/11 with zero skips. Live treasury balance/supply deltas and supply-key authorization remain unverified pending funded testnet credentials. Other providers' adoption of the new amount field and affected API clients require verification; their previous whole-balance semantics are not proof of explicit-amount support.

The provider transaction-status method queries the configured consensus network through the SDK's `TransactionReceiptQuery`. Thirteen recognized terminal contract, signature, account, token and NFT failure statuses map to `Failed`; existing success, pending, expiry and fee/balance classifications are retained. Unclassified, malformed and precheck-only statuses remain errors rather than fabricated ledger outcomes. The native status is retained in the response message. All thirteen failure names match the installed official SDK's status definitions. Focused mapping tests pass 16/16 with zero skips in `TestResults/HederaRuntime/hedera-receipt-status.trx`; the SDK bridge suites remain 39/39. These are offline checks, not confirmed failed-transaction consensus evidence. A funded-testnet transfer and independent receipt-query integration test is implemented and compiles, but remains unexecuted.

`Scripts/TestHosts/Get-ReusableHederaTestIdentity.ps1` generates one ECDSA key with the existing pinned SDK and retains it outside source control, encrypted with current-user Windows DPAPI. Subsequent runs reuse the same public EVM alias. A testnet mirror lookup returned 404, so account creation and funding are not established. The official anonymous faucet accepts an account ID or EVM address, but browser control failed before opening it; no funding request was submitted. Funded tests still require `OASIS_HEDERA_TEST_ACCOUNT_ID`, `OASIS_HEDERA_TEST_PRIVATE_KEY` (DER) and `OASIS_HEDERA_TEST_RECIPIENT_ID`. No local Hedera runtime, additional SDK or browser was installed.

OASISDNA `HashgraphOASIS.ConsensusNodes` maps explicit loopback consensus endpoints to numeric node account IDs and is forwarded by the bootloader. `Network = local` requires this map; public networks reject custom nodes rather than silently changing their destination. The constructor copies the map to isolate subsequent caller changes. DNA, HashgraphOASIS and bootloader bounded builds passed with zero errors. These configuration tests do not start a consensus node or prove its ledger identity.

HashgraphOASIS remains **MISSING** in the complete real-runtime matrix. These tests perform no chain writes and do not prove live consensus transfers. Funded testnet credentials or an approved reusable local consensus runtime are required for that gate. SDK deployment packaging, avatar wallet resolution, storage, NFT and remaining token lifecycle operations still need implementation or verification. Mirror reads are not proof of provider activation or mutation support.

### Wallet credential isolation

Hedera avatar-to-avatar transfers by ID, username or email now resolve the sender's default signing wallet and the recipient's public wallet, then use the existing signed SDK HBAR/HTS transfer path. Invalid amounts, identities and token IDs fail before wallet lookup. The current Hedera offline filter passes 59/59 tests with zero skips, including six new avatar-transfer validation cases; the earlier 53-test storage checkpoint above remains a subset. Actual wallet resolution and funded consensus submission remain live gates. The bare-address legacy `SendTransactionAsync` path has not yet been migrated.

Core commit `ab43dfe99136bed894967a1b78984e3d93da551a` extends detached decryption to general wallet-list filtering as well as default-wallet ID, username and email lookups. List filtering clones wallet responses before decryption; an absent provider returns an empty dictionary. Malformed private-key or recovery-phrase ciphertext fails the list operation instead of silently continuing, and failed ById responses clear the payload. The focused isolation suite passes 13/13 with zero skips in `TestResults/TronRuntime/wallet-credential-safety.trx`, covering the earlier default-wallet cases and valid/invalid list ciphertext without mutating cached originals. V2 routing contracts pass 183/183 with zero skips in `TestResults/HyperDriveContracts/v2-operation-contracts.trx`; wallet assertions require equivalent detached results while preserving provider-selection and async invocation checks. These controlled-provider tests do not prove authenticated live credential routes.

The async default-wallet lookup now reads encrypted wallet objects and decrypts a detached response through the existing credential helper. A decryption failure returns an error without a wallet payload. The concrete wallet copy preserves all scalar identity and holon fields; referenced collections remain shared because credential processing does not modify them. API Core commit `10348ae783c6ebe78cf513d33bf7b9ff01a87282` contains this change. Other wallet lookup/filter paths still need review for in-place decryption; this checkpoint does not claim that every credential path is safe.

Focused wallet credential and transfer-request tests passed **6/6, zero skips**, recorded in `TestResults/TronRuntime/wallet-credential-safety.trx`. These include the public async default-wallet lookup with private-key display disabled and with decryption failure, original credential preservation, detached identity preservation, and provider request field mapping. The lookup tests use a controlled storage provider, not a live avatar account.

### Reusable environment and disk cleanup

The shared wallet manager previously discarded `Web3TokenId`, `FromTokenAddress`, `OwnerPrivateKey`, `OwnerPublicKey` and `OwnerSeedPhrase` while constructing a provider send request. Its common request mapper now preserves those fields along with addresses, amount and memo. The asynchronous WEB4 send wrapper also propagates a failed or missing provider response instead of leaving a successful empty result. The request-mapping tests now pass as part of the six-test wallet checkpoint above. This does not yet prove implicit avatar-wallet signing, real provider error propagation through the HTTP route or multi-provider orchestration.

The task continues in `C:\Source\OASIS-hyperdrive-v2-gaps` without new worktrees or duplicate SDK environments. The hosted Mongo runner now uses a locked fixed `runs/current` directory, overwrites bounded per-run logs and removes its disposable databases after owned processes stop, retaining logs and TRX reports. The revised runner passed 43/43 transaction/election tests and 1/1 abrupt-primary-termination test on 2026-10-08, with zero skips; it exited successfully and all three owned database directories were absent afterward. Reports are retained in `TestResults/HostedMongoRelease/evidence`.

Unbounded .NET builds retained 70 MSBuild workers using approximately 9 GiB of RAM. With no active .NET CLI operation, `dotnet build-server shutdown` stopped the idle servers. Subsequent builds/tests use `/m:2 /nr:false /p:UseSharedCompilation=false` to bound workers and avoid retaining server processes. Free C: space rose from approximately 90 GiB to 101 GiB after shutdown; no pagefile or user files were deleted. This transient free-space change is not counted as additional artifact cleanup.

Cleanup removed approximately 16.5 GiB of verified disposable Mongo archives/debug symbols, inactive synthetic Mongo databases, Antelope code caches and generated Holochain native-test output. Authorized WSL trim/shutdown/compaction reduced the Ubuntu VHD from about 69.6 GiB to 45.9 GiB, reclaiming a further 23.7 GiB without deleting Linux user data. Source, uncommitted work, credentials, packaged Holochain output and unrelated environments were retained. This accounts for about 40 GiB reclaimed, not the entire 60–70 GiB the user requested. Remaining disk usage still needs investigation; deleting user data or unrelated environments is not authorized.

The 2026-10-11 disk audit measured roughly 14.6 GiB of build outputs across the task, primary and resilience checkouts, 2 GiB in stopped Antelope synthetic ledgers and a 58.4 GiB WSL disk. Removal of the two validated Antelope ledger directories was rejected by the execution tool, so this session did not reclaim those files. Two HoloNET Android emulators created on 2026-10-10 and their SDK accounted for approximately 32 GiB; their folders and process disappeared during the audit while free C: space increased from approximately 55 GiB to 80 GiB. Those removals were external to this session. The existing Unity Android SDK remains available; no replacement was downloaded. After the user confirmed cleanup had finished, bounded provider builds resumed and required dependencies were restored into the existing shared NuGet cache. A subsequent measurement showed 93.55 GiB free on C:. The user's 150 GB free-space target has not been met. Earlier reports removed by external cleanup require fresh verification before they can serve as retained evidence.

### SDK bridge packaging verification

Cosmos, Hedera and NEAR previously copied their distinct bridges to the same `SdkBridge/bridge.mjs` host-output path. Their defaults and MSBuild output/publish links now use `CosmosSdkBridge`, `HederaSdkBridge` and `NearSdkBridge` respectively, matching Sui's already-separated `SuiSdkBridge` convention. All three affected providers built with zero errors, and each copied bridge's hash matches its own source. Sui also explicitly preserves its bridge and lockfiles in publish output. Source-path environment overrides remain supported for the one reusable development installation.

All seven WEB4–WEB10 Dockerfiles now supply official Node 24.19.0 from the verified Docker Hub manifest `sha256:a9f5f7c91a432850b2a8a7797adf5eadb6c733ceed61167806cee7ea7fbc29df`. Their build stage runs `Docker/package-provider-sdk-bridges.sh`, which only accepts the Docker-owned `/app/publish` directory, rejects incomplete bridge artifacts, runs `npm ci --omit=dev` for each included provider and verifies the SDK subpath imports used by its bridge. Final images include Node and execute a version check. The existing dependency-manifest validator enforces these instructions in every Dockerfile. Shell syntax, all four SDK import sets against existing installations and the seven-file manifest policy passed; no additional runtime or container image was downloaded locally. Full image builds, Linux dependency compatibility, published default-path bridge execution and deployed API routes remain verification gates.

### Real-provider V2 orchestration gate

`CosmosRealProviderRoutingTests` passed **2/2, zero skips**, on 2026-10-11 against the retained local wasmd contract; replication/selection evidence is `TestResults/HyperDriveCosmos/cosmos-v2-routing.trx`. The V2 router saves through LocalFile and replicates to Cosmos, then independently reads the holon from both stores and checks successful replication diagnostics. Performance selection uses measured reads from both real stores and verifies that the chosen provider's request count increases. The separate failover run passed **2/2, zero skips**, in 44 seconds; `TestResults/HyperDriveCosmos/cosmos-v2-outage.trx` covers both SDK transport closure and actual wasmd process shutdown after provider activation. Each case checks secondary LocalFile persistence and the selected-provider diagnostic. The shutdown helper validates the exact retained container ID, pinned image and running state before sending SIGINT to wasmd, then confirms node exit without escalating signals. The node exited before the idle container was stopped; its ledger and deployed contract are preserved. This does not establish recovery after restart, every operation, HTTP routes or every provider combination. Tests use the fixed, exclusively locked `TestResults/HyperDriveCosmos` directory, existing contract and public local development keys; owned LocalFile records are deleted through their provider.

`NextGenSoftware.OASIS.API.Core.HyperDrive.IntegrationTests` runs the V2 router against isolated LocalFile and SQLite stores, not provider mocks. Its 3/3 zero-skip gate proves that a failed primary mutation is written by the real secondary provider, a successful mutation is persisted and readable from both configured replicas, and performance selection consumes latency measured from actual provider reads. The SQLite provider now has an explicit disposal lifecycle so repeatable tests and hosts can close its real database handle deterministically.

The Sui replication and measured-selection run passed **2/2, zero skips**, in `TestResults/HyperDriveSui/sui-v2-routing.trx`. It proves a routed save replicated from LocalFile into the actual Sui contract, readback from both stores, successful replication diagnostics, and performance selection following measured reads from those real providers. The separate failover run passed **2/2, zero skips**, in `TestResults/HyperDriveSui/sui-v2-outage.trx`, covering owned SDK transport closure and actual shutdown of the retained local Sui validator/fullnode process after provider activation. Both cases persist the routed mutation in LocalFile and report LocalFile as the selected failover provider. The shutdown helper requires an explicit PID, verifies the exact pinned executable and retained network configuration, sends SIGINT and confirms process exit; it refuses unrelated processes and sends no escalation signal. The retained ledger is not reset or copied. The node is stopped after the run. Tests reuse a locked fixed directory and delete their own LocalFile holons. Recovery after restart, other operation types, deployed HTTP routes and the all-provider orchestration gate remain open.

### Railway OASISDNA mode configuration

Railway holds the complete DNA document in the `OASIS_DNA_JSON` service variable; the mode was not changed in a repository JSON file. The safe update procedure was:

1. read the existing variable from the exact Railway project, environment and WEB4 service;
2. parse it in memory;
3. set `OASIS.HyperDriveMode` to the exact value `OASISHyperDrive2`;
4. update `OASIS.OASISHyperDriveConfig` while preserving all unrelated keys and provider credentials;
5. serialize the complete document and pipe it directly to Railway's variable command through standard input;
6. verify the redeployed `/api/hyperdrive/mode`, `/config` and `/status` endpoints.

Secrets were neither printed nor committed. Changing `OASIS_DNA.json` locally would not change Railway because `OASIS_DNA_JSON` is the deployed source of truth. Likewise, changing only `OASIS.HyperDriveMode` is insufficient if `OASIS.OASISHyperDriveConfig` does not define the intended V2 failover, replication and load-balancing policy.

## October 11 verification checkpoint (supersedes narrower counts above)

`OASISHederaTokenEscrow.sol` adds depositor-owned fungible HTS custody on top of the same NFT escrow base and reentrancy guard. Since the fungible lock request has no amount, `lockAll` deposits the caller's full spendable positive int64 balance, performs HIP-719 association when needed, and records exact units. Deposit and release verify both sides' balance changes; false/inexact transfers revert rather than recording success. Unlock returns only the caller's recorded deposit to that caller. No administrator release path is added.

`node Scripts/TestHosts/verify-hedera-token-escrow-evm.cjs` passes **28 ordinary-EVM assertions** with the installed solc 0.8.26/Hardhat runtime: allowance requirement, association/transfer failure, inexact-credit rejection, atomic state/allowance rollback, outsider rejection, failed-release preservation, successful custody/release and deployed runtime hash consistency. Its current compiled runtime hash is `0xd8684d4b39c44da80c7f9de3080ba315b453e0bb4f14b59277c6665ac0cbc360`. This is not a Hedera deployment or live HTS result. The C# fungible methods still require signed SDK allowance/query/execution wiring, explicit configured deployment/code hash and live validation; the hard-coded one-token/default-account legacy implementation is not counted as complete. The existing browser tool was rechecked for official-faucet access but fails before a page opens (`failed to write kernel assets`, missing path); no new browser/runtime installation was performed.

The SDK process boundary now preserves both originally allocated mutation and possible approval IDs on cancellation/timeout, without claiming an approval completed when the process outcome is unknown. Caller cancellation retains its cancellation token; pre-cancellation fails before process launch. Error guidance explicitly states that terminating a process does not cancel a ledger submission. Two bounded real-Node-process fixture tests pass **2/2, zero skips** (`TestResults/HederaRuntime/hedera-process-boundary.trx`): structured failed-process metadata retains confirmed approval/mutation/stage, and caller cancellation retains both unverified IDs without key leakage. The fixtures have no SDK/network/signing operations and are not funded provider evidence. The two-minute timeout branch shares reconciliation construction but was not itself waited out in these tests.

Escrow now requires an explicit `NftEscrowRuntimeCodeHash` in DNA/bootloader/provider requests. The SDK queries executable contract bytecode and compares its Keccak-256 hash before approval or mutation. The fixed local compiler/EVM script verifies compiled deployed-bytecode hash against actual local deployment code: **21 assertions pass**, currently yielding `0x74bae5130b7161d734258f31ccad0171ce1da7a3b85727bd0c5dc7b296818f80` for its exact solc 0.8.26/Paris/source settings. This is not a Hedera deployment hash; deployment must use and independently verify the matching artifact, not copy an unrelated hash.

Before unlock and after lock readback, the recorded EVM depositor is resolved through a real SDK alias account query and must identify the exact active numeric requesting account. No EVM identity is guessed from the current signing key or the regular account's `contractAccountId` (the [consensus protobuf contract](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hiero-ledger/hiero-consensus-node/main/hapi/hedera-protobuf-java-api/src/main/proto/services/crypto_get_info.proto) reserves that field for smart contracts). SDK helper/dispatcher tests now pass **58/58, zero skips**; the combined non-funded C# suite passes **123/123, zero skips** (`TestResults/HederaRuntime/hedera-nonfunded.trx`), including missing/malformed code-hash rejection before wallet lookup. DNA/provider/bootloader builds pass. The code-hash and EVM-alias query execution branches still require funded live verification, along with deployment, allowance/readback and multikey/route authorization. These additions supersede the implementation gaps for code pinning and depositor binding, not their live-verification gates.

The combined current non-funded Hedera C# suite passes **120/120, zero skips** in `TestResults/HederaRuntime/hedera-nonfunded.trx` (`FullyQualifiedName~Hedera&FullyQualifiedName!~HederaTestnet`). It reuses the installed SDK and includes the identity, history, status, request-validation and mirror-read checks; funded transfer/storage tests are deliberately excluded, not silently counted as passed or skipped. This is not the missing funded V2 orchestration evidence.

Hedera C# `LockNFTAsync`/`UnlockNFTAsync` now invoke the real escrow SDK dispatcher, resolving the requesting avatar's detached default signing wallet rather than looking up NFT data and fabricating a lock result. DNA and bootloader expose independent `NftEscrowContractId`, `NftEscrowGasLimit`, and `NftEscrowMaxFeeTinybars`; no implicit contract/gas/fee defaults authorize execution. Invalid configuration, missing avatar identity and malformed/out-of-range asset addresses fail before wallet lookup. Successful responses must identify the exact originally requested mutation ID and successful receipt/readback status; a completed approval ID is retained in the response message. The focused boundary suite passes **10/10, zero skips** (`TestResults/HederaRuntime/hedera-nft-escrow-validation.trx`). DNA, provider and bootloader builds succeed. Funded execution, wallet-route authorization, contract code identity, exact EVM-depositor binding and multikey support remain unverified. This supersedes earlier 'C# lock/unlock not wired' checkpoints; it does not close the live escrow gate. Fungible lock/unlock still reference the old unconfigured contract field and remain a separate implementation gap.

The SDK dispatcher now supports escrow lock/unlock execution using real account/contract/NFT queries, single-serial native HTS approval, signed contract execution and successful receipt plus custody/deposit readback. Single-key sender identity is verified from consensus account information; key-list/multisignature accounts are explicitly not handled by this path. Lock refuses an existing different spender rather than replacing its allowance silently. Approval and mutation have distinct original IDs and no automatic resubmission. Error transport and the C# SDK boundary retain confirmed approval ID, original mutation ID and failure stage for reconciliation. The escrow module is copied into build/publish output beside the bridge. Provider build succeeds; the current SDK suite passes **56/56, zero skips** (including dispatcher rejection before funded queries). These tests do **not** execute the funded query/submission/readback path. Exact payer-to-EVM depositor binding, configured contract code identity, multisignature support, live HTS approval/custody lifecycle and C# lock/unlock wiring remain gates. This supersedes the earlier 'builders not wired into dispatcher' checkpoint, not its live-verification limitation.

The escrow SDK construction layer (`SdkBridge/escrow.mjs`) now builds real `ContractExecuteTransaction` lock/unlock calls and a single-serial `AccountAllowanceApproveTransaction`. It validates numeric nonzero payer/contract/token IDs, exact positive int64 serials, explicit gas and fee ceilings, and separate approval/deposit transaction IDs for the same payer. Contract calldata and allowance protobuf round-trip through the installed SDK without losing serials above JavaScript safe-integer precision. Client-local one-attempt/no-ID-regeneration settings are asserted on the original submission objects (these SDK settings are not protobuf fields). The complete existing SDK suite passes **53/53, zero skips**, including 14 escrow construction cases. No paid query or transaction is executed by these tests. These builders are not yet wired into the bridge dispatcher or C# lock/unlock; payer/EVM binding, signing/execution, state readback, partial-approval reconciliation and live funded validation remain required.

The checked-in `HashgraphOASIS/Contracts/OASISHederaNftEscrow.sol` defines depositor-owned HTS NFT custody, validates positive int64 serials, verifies ownership before deposit and after each transfer, and rejects arbitrary release recipients. It checks token association through the official [HIP-719 interface](https://github.com/hiero-ledger/hiero-contracts/blob/main/contracts/token-service/IHRC719.sol) before accepting custody. Association and transfer failures revert the whole lock; failed unlock preserves the deposit and custody. No administrator release function exists.

`node Scripts/TestHosts/verify-hedera-nft-escrow-evm.cjs` reuses installed solc 0.8.26, Hardhat and ethers without downloads or a filesystem ledger. Its **20 assertions pass** against an explicit ordinary-EVM token fixture: serial bounds, owner checks, missing approval, failed association/deposit/withdrawal rollback, outsider rejection and successful relock/release. This is **not live HTS emulation or consensus evidence**. The provider's current lock/unlock methods are not yet wired to this contract; SDK allowance/query/execution, configurable deployment identity, payer/EVM-address binding, funded deployment and live HTS lifecycle remain required before escrow is complete.

Hedera NFT bridge withdrawal/deposit now delegate to the existing real signed HTS `SendNFTAsync` route, transferring one exact token/serial between the explicit sender and configured pool (or pool and recipient). They no longer submit a zero-value HBAR transaction through the legacy helper. Token-only plus explicit serial and complete token/serial addresses must agree; malformed, nonpositive, overflowing or conflicting serials fail before pool credentials are resolved. Native/NFT bridge account validation has one shared owner. The combined bridge validation suite now passes **16/16, zero skips** (`TestResults/HederaRuntime/hedera-native-bridge.trx`), superseding the nine-case native-only checkpoint below.

NFT bridge success establishes only the HTS transfer receipt, not an escrow contract, source-chain proof, mint/burn settlement or release authorization. `sourceTransactionHash` is not treated as authorization. Funded HTS bridge transfers, token association/receiver-signature requirements, host authorization and dedicated NFT lock/unlock escrow implementation remain gates. No funded NFT was moved during these validation tests.

Hedera native `WithdrawAsync`/`DepositAsync` now use the same real signed SDK HBAR transfer path as `SendTokenAsync`, not the legacy unsigned helper. They require an explicit nonzero numeric `BridgePoolAccountId`, explicit different sender/recipient, positive exact int64 tinybars and a real DER signing key. Pool release resolves the configured `BridgePoolSigningKeyEnvironmentVariable` only after request validation; DNA stores the variable name, never the key. The bootloader forwards both independent pool settings. No storage contract or fabricated `0.0.0` destination is reused. Completed describes the confirmed native-chain leg only, not cross-chain settlement.

Native bridge validation tests pass **9/9, zero skips** in `TestResults/HederaRuntime/hedera-native-bridge.trx`, covering absent/invalid pool, zero/negative/fractional/overflow amounts, absent signer and self/invalid recipients, including no credential reads for rejected requests. These tests perform no funded submission. Real pool transfers, authenticated pool-release authorization, settlement orchestration and NFT escrow remain open gates; do not expose pool release to untrusted callers without the required host authorization. The later native-transfer implementation supersedes the legacy native bridge gap noted below, but not the remaining NFT paths.

Hedera provider `CreateAccountAsync` and `RestoreAccountAsync` now use the installed Hiero SDK's 12-word mnemonic generation/validation and standard Ed25519 derivation at index 0, returning DER keys compatible with the existing signed SDK operations. Local identity operations do not require storage activation and honor pre-cancellation. Their messages explicitly distinguish signing identities from funded ledger-account creation/linkage; no account ID is fabricated. The real installed-SDK C# tests pass **3/3, zero skips** (`TestResults/HederaRuntime/hedera-sdk-keys.trx`), including generated mnemonic recovery, DER interoperability, invalid words/checksum and cancellation without credentials.

**Migration boundary:** the old public `HashgraphClient` helper remains legacy and is not changed. Its BIP39 seed-prefix/base64 derivation is not the standard SDK mnemonic scheme. Existing legacy wallet keys are not rewritten; a legacy mnemonic must not be assumed to restore the same key through the standard provider method. Explicit legacy-key migration/recovery and funded on-chain account creation remain open gates. Likewise, inspection found the provider's NFT lock/unlock and bridge deposit/withdraw paths still call legacy helpers and cannot serve as real V2 escrow/settlement evidence; these require real signed implementations and live verification before completion.

`Scripts/TestHosts/Get-ReusableHederaTestIdentity.ps1 -CheckLedger` reuses the same DPAPI-protected identity and performs a bounded, read-only testnet mirror account lookup with redirects disabled. It validates account identity, active state and integer tinybar balance if present, but does not equate a mirror balance with consensus/signing verification.
